# Psykinetic roadmap

Last updated: 2026-10-14. Owner: Jeff. Every session reads this before starting work.
The player-facing version (for Dire) lives in a separate doc; this one is for building.

## Goal right now

The foundations work (co-op server, zones, physics combat, talking companions, level
editor, art pipeline). What's missing is a reason to play: something to find, something
you become, someone to save. Everything below serves the first scenario: rescue the smith.

## Top priority: builder mode (`builder` worktree)

Real-time map building in the game, driven by the Claude API, so places with things to do
can be made quickly. This is the most important track right now.

| Pass | Job | Done when |
|------|-----|-----------|
| 1 | **Canvas and stamps** | Editor resize (grow/shrink from any side; entities and level.json shift to match). A 256 × 256 outdoor map `levels/wilds` with forest edges. Prefabs: select an area in the editor, save as a named prefab (terrain, edges, entities, idle behaviours) under `levels/prefabs/`, stamp anywhere with rotation. Seed a few by hand: small house, campfire camp (gather points), fenced field, well. |
| 2 | **Area tools** | Usable by hand in the editor: pond (water blob with shore), grove (scattered trees, optional clearing), path between clicked points (winding, with width), fence/wall line, scatter (rocks, crates, bushes). Each with size and seed, and undo. |
| 3 | **Builder mode** | In-game dev toggle (B): drag to select an area, type a request ("a pond here", "a goblin camp guarding a chest"). The server sends the area, its contents, the legend and the prefab list to Claude (key in `/etc/psykinetic/env`, never in the client or repo) with tools for paint terrain, path, place entity (monsters with idle behaviour, chests with contents), stamp prefab, and the area tools. Edits show as a ghost preview; Enter accepts, Esc discards, follow-ups refine ("bigger", "move it north"). Accepted edits go through the editor's undo and save to the map files. Log cost per request; spend limit set in the Console. |
| 4 | **New area at an edge** | Needs the world map from `maps` (edge links, the area graph). Builder tool `new_area(side, size, description)`: when the selection touches a map edge, create a new level with forest edges, link both ways through `neighbours` (walk off one edge, arrive at the matching edge at the same position), optionally draft it from the description, load it live. Undo removes area and link. |

It should place *things to do*, not just scenery: chests with loot, camps to clear,
captives, puzzles, larders. The first thing built with it: the farmstead for the smith scenario.

Coordinates and codes (Jeff, 2026-10-19: "in build mode it could mark coordinates and name buildings with a code?"): in builder mode the HUD shows the cursor's and the player's cell, every building wears a short stable code ("H3"), the hint and a click name it with its cell and facing, and the codes serve as targets in builder requests; so a play-test note can say where.

Passes 1–4 are on main (with heights 356 and roofs 374). Next (approved 2026-10-19): builder
catches up with what other trees added since pass 3, each its own landing: monster kinds and
their idle behaviours read from data (orc, kobold, wildlife) with a legend in words; chest
contents from items' loot tables or explicit items checked against the catalog; a stream tool on
maps' 505 (water, ford, bridge); buildings' 488 kinds and sizes through set_building; captives
and larders only with a marker that exists on main (none does, 2026-10-19: a level-placed captive is economy's with npc, larders unassigned; both deferred until trade and sickness settle). Monster kinds 507, loot tables 509 and streams 510 landed 2026-10-19, the composed buildings 511 landing. Places and holdings (builder's design, approved 2026-10-19, three owners): maps adds
work-site place kinds (woodlot, hunting_ground, quarry; farm and mine count) and an optional
"town" key naming the stockpile a site works for; economy, after phase 3, makes a trade work
from its town's site of its kind (no site: today's timer yield; a held site: the yield and the
worker walking there on the slow tick; a site held by raiders or contested: no yield and the
town uneasy), so raiders on the woodlot stop the wood; builder then marks sites from a request
("a woodcutter's camp here"). Set pieces once built. Nothing in a real level until Jeff asks.

## Order of work (combat and characters)

Each step is what the next one needs. Weapons only feel good if you can read them;
a story only matters if there's something to fight with.

| # | Step | Done when | Worktree |
|---|------|-----------|----------|
| 1 | **Performance** | 256² map: load < 2 s (3D build included), server tick < 20 ms at castle density with many players spread over the map (Jeff: "needs to accommodate larger numbers"; benched at 1, 4, 9 and 16 players), 60 fps on the laptop; per-frame scripts < 4 ms at 128², < 8 ms at 256². Far-away monsters think less often (full rate within ~16 cells of a player/companion, about a screen; one tick in 4 out to 60; fights always full rate, wherever they are). Beyond that, and in zones nobody is in, the world is not paused: it moves on at a slow world tick (see "The world moves on" below). | `perf` Priority (Jeff, 2026-10-17: "performance is suffering lately yes"): a regression hunt, box ticks per version from the journal and client frame time on main against the v0.1.145 and v0.1.148 builds in the same scenes, suspects the new skinned animals and people, the tree stands, the ui panels; the server names its slow ticks by phase. Explained (2026-10-19, C:\dev\review\perf\regression.txt): the v0.1.149 → v0.1.150 step was a host's minimap drawn every frame (a fix for a blank map, now redrawn only on change, about 0.2 ms); the rest is new features, not one bug (the animation rig's skinned bodies and shadows about 0.5 ms, per-body bow, held-item and bar updates 0.2–0.5 ms, less ui's 476 saving), nothing to revert at 2.2 ms a frame against 1.35 on v0.1.148; two hitches sent to their owners (sound's music check reading the disk four times a second, 2–4 ms; dungeon's regeneration re-parsing every level.json each world tick, 3.8 ms); the server's first-tick and death-route stalls fixed. Next: re-profile after those two, then the server-boot export test (450). |
| 2 | **Network smoothness** | No rubber-banding in a castle fight. Knockback sent as one event (direction, distance, start tick) every client plays identically; others drawn ~100–150 ms in the past and interpolated; corrections blended, not snapped; camera follows a smoothed position. Report corrections/min and worst snap before/after. | after `perf` (same client code) |
| 3 | **Combat animation states** | Synced from sim state: wind-up (longer for heavy attacks), strike, recover, hit (direction-aware), pushed (matches knockback event), knocked down (fall/lie/get up, timed to sim), block, guard break, death. Clean blends, no popping. | after `pc-character` |
| 4 | **Player character** | One shared humanoid body for players and companions, human proportions so the animation library fits, hand attach points for weapons. Replaces the capsule. Then (Jeff, 2026-10-17, after rejecting every image-to-mesh body: "we need to work on original creation methods because I envision having numerous models for town characters, different postures, statures, builds, hair etc."): a character generator of our own, a base body authored once and varied by parameters (stature, build, posture, age, face planes) with modular hair, beard and clothes on the palette, deterministic per person, rigged and paced automatically, so players, companions and every townsperson get a body of their own. Monster bases from the same generator (approved 2026-10-18, on lore's 416: brutes are orcs, sneaks are kobolds): `--people orc|kobold`, joints moved for proportion before building so every humanoid clip and socket still fits; the orc about 1.95 m, broad, tusked, hunched, in harness and loincloth; the kobold about 1.0 m, crouched, long-armed with digging claws, snout, short tail, miner's cap and pick; skins toward the Blood Rot's green; brute and sneak draw them (the imp stays the goblin); a review sheet beside a human and a goblin, then a clip. People (Jeff, 2026-10-18: "the people arent great either but it works better than cylinders"): the generator stays the working base; once art-gen's goblin recipe (image to mesh with a painted texture) holds for the monsters, a human base is tried by it, variation as texture and parts on top. Idle fidgets (Jeff, 2026-10-18: "the idle need variability and same scratch is very predictable"): several fidget clips per body picked at random, the head scratch rare, timing seeded so no person repeats at the same second each run. Vetoed on the sheet (Jeff, 2026-10-18: "not happy with orc and kobold, have image regenerate with confyui 10 candidates each. orc should be a grey blue, kobold a light red to purple with scales and fur"): art-gen makes 10 concepts each, the orc grey-blue, the kobold light red to purple with scales and fur; pc-character restyles the bases to Jeff's picks. Route (Jeff, 2026-10-19: "the brute in game model is still pretty clunky ... a hand redo by me in blender"; then "the trellis retarget is what's close, not the blue blob monster"): the mesh route, the TRELLIS retargets land as the orc and the kobold with their painted textures through the goblin's shader path, under the veto rule; the generator stays the fallback and the people's base; a Blender kit for Jeff's own redo (C:\dev\review\orc_hand\, the skeleton in T-pose, the retarget as the start body, references hidden, everything packed), handed back as orc.blend and weighted by pc-character. Then (Jeff, 2026-10-19: "even the rebuilt orc and kobold need a lot of tlc. i worked in blender for a couple hours on clean up - thats gonna slow us down"): two routes tried at once, Jeff picks from the sheet: art-kit hand-models the orc from the concept in big clean planes over the kit's armature, as the props were rebuilt; pc-character adds an automated cleanup stage after TRELLIS (remesh to clean quads, the paint re-projected, holes and slivers gone) measured on the orc and kobold, with Jeff's own cleaned file as the list of what it must fix. | `pc-character` |
| 5 | **Weapon models** | Hammer, sword, shield, giant axe, spear, dagger, bow — flat, faceted style, attach to hand bones. | art session (Blender MCP) |

| 7 | **The smith scenario** | A map and story to use it all in (see below). | later |

### The world moves on (design rule for the performance work)

Activity while players are away or out of range is part of the game (camps grow, captives
are moved, enemies hold and retake ground; see the backlog's offline consequences). So
nothing far away is simply paused, but it doesn't need the 10 Hz sim either:

- **Near players:** the full tick, as now.
- **Far, or a zone with nobody in it:** a slow world tick (seconds to minutes apart) that
  moves things on in coarse steps: who holds what, where groups are, what has grown or
  been taken. No pathfinding cell by cell and no hits.
- **When players arrive:** the area is settled from where the world tick left it (on wake,
  where `World._woke` logs the time asleep today), then runs at the full tick.
- **Offline:** the world tick keeps running on the server with no one connected, at the
  same coarse rate.

- **Movement is real** (Jeff, 2026-10-14): groups on the world tick walk real paths at their
  own speed, never jump, inside areas as well as between them.
- **Held ground is physical:** a group can't route through ground another faction holds
  (the cells within a held place's radius; contested ground blocks everyone). It goes
  around, or it meets the holders and the world tick makes the encounter (fight or take).
- **Server downtime pauses the world:** the world tick's clock is saved and resumes where it
  stopped; nothing is caught up for the time the server was down. Players being offline
  pauses nothing.

Perf should leave room for this when it builds the far-monster cut-off: the far tier
hands over to the world tick, not to a stop.

## Running in parallel (not on the critical path)

| Worktree | Job |
|----------|-----|
| `companions` | **Companion minds and verbs** (Jeff, 2026-10-14): owns Wren, Pip, Nix and future companions (how they perceive, speak and act); one perception layer shared with `enemy-ai` (and later town NPCs). In order, shipping each: (1) speech polish from recent transcripts: the repetition filter compares normalized text and catches near-identical lines; quieter after minor events, speaks when something new happens; never narrates the event log or refers to its own player in third person; check the castle-playtest issues are gone (ordering other players' companions, Nix collapsing into one-word fragments, a companion answering a line meant for another's); (2) the shared perception layer (see by light, hear by distance, remember what was witnessed); (3) real verbs the voice can choose: OPEN/CLOSE DOOR (lock/bar once doors support it), OPEN CHEST (opens and reports contents), LOOK (check a nearby dark or unseen spot, report back, return); the voice only promises actions it can do; (4) bandaging an adjacent ally out of combat and handing an item over, matching the player's bandaging; (5) always-on world: companions in zones with no players nearby make no voice calls; (6) card system: trait tables rolled at creation, one model call writes the card, a "how we met" field; prompts say what's true and what to do, never what to say. Experiment (go-ahead given, Jeff 2026-10-17): a Claude API voice (Haiku/Sonnet) with fallback to the box's 14B, comparing transcripts, latency and cost per 100 lines. Who may say what (Jeff, 2026-10-17): "offical characters only speak lore facts, priests, guards, officials -- bards, scoundrels, and thieves aren't bound to lore facts -- they can embellish and lie but only in character, and towns people don't fabricate they can store what they hear and decide, they may have a intillect stat that influences how vulnerable they are to falsehoods": roles carry a binding (official: agreed lore only; free: may embellish and lie in character; townsfolk: never fabricate, keep what they are told with its source and decide by an intellect stat whether to believe and repeat it as hearsay). Companions keep the card rule (what she makes up becomes a fact about herself, never about the world). Then (Jeff, 2026-10-17): "lets give them each a knowledge domain, medicine man domain: nature, supersition: high, creativity: low (doesn't make up his own lore) intellect: high (not vulnerable to falsehoods) integrity: high -- opppsite would be theif low integrity, high creativity domain: commerce and goods": each role has a profile: a knowledge domain (which lore it knows), superstition, creativity (whether it makes up lore), intellect (how vulnerable to falsehoods) and integrity (whether it lies); the official/free/townsfolk binding is derived from creativity and integrity, not a separate flag; individuals roll around their role's profile. "companions should be able to see NPC the same as enemies but marked as friendly" (Jeff, 2026-10-17): the perception layer reports residents and other companions in sight as it does monsters, as friendly, by name and trade. Doors (Jeff, 2026-10-17): "companions tend to stand in doorways, when i move into building they should consider, doorway plugging a battle strategy not a routine": following never stops on a door cell; holding a doorway is a fight-time choice only; "step aside" and a bump move her out of the way. Done per step: a short before/after excerpt from a real or replayed transcript. Jeff's loyalty ruling (2026-10-22 night, his words, verbatim in the companions tree's message): tiers slower, each with its own deeds that can't raise past it (fond to close: everyday warmth: gifts, food, fire together, knucklebones, rest in town, days on roads; close to devoted: trust under pressure: keeping watch while she naps, a new ability, tending her when badly hurt, fights won together; devoted: a milestone only: a rescue, an area finished together, the area's companion reward); lower-tier deeds repair lost points within the tier; no decay; tiers change her behaviour (wary hesitates and refuses risky orders, close follows, devoted takes the lead when asked and protects aggressively); damage never costs loyalty, the low-health drop replaced by -1 for pressing into a new fight while she's under 1/3 health after she asked to rest, cleared by rest or a bandage; the pack drop once until she rests; harsh words, gifts and food kept, struck gone. And his priority, from Dire's play test ("his 'wow' moments were Nix acting on plain words (bandaging when told) and finding she could carry gear"): "Companions are the centerpiece; reliable plain-language commands for the core verbs (bandage, carry, wait, rest, take the lead) come before combat polish.": companions' queue is the core verbs first, each reliable from plain words with a test that speaks ten phrasings per verb, before 587's remaining sides, the games and the rest; its plan came the same night and is approved: a hands' reader that reads the player's own line for the core verbs with no model involved and acts on the next tick (her voice still asked, told what she's doing, speaking in her words; unrecognised lines to the model as today; the reader wins over the model): bandage, carry (a named item from his pack to hers, give back), wait (HOLD), follow (STAY_CLOSE/GUARD), rest (a new REST: sits and mends faster out of a fight, stands when a foe comes, "after this" in one), take the lead (the guide to a named place, else the nearest town); reliable = a suite companion_test section with scripted minds: ten varied phrasings per verb acting within 1 tick, five near-misses that must not act, a never-answering mind (the act happens, a stock acknowledgement in her temperament after 4 s), a contradicting mind (the act stands, the contradicting line dropped), tier gates; and once, a live check of 60 lines through the box's Ollama tunnel; built now, before 587's remaining sides, knucklebones and the tiers; number from release. The tier table (warmth +0.5 deeds to 3, trust +1 deeds to 6, milestones +2 at devoted, repair to best-ever within the tier, no decay, the drops, behaviour by tier) approved as the draft and in his folder as draft-loyalty-tiers.md with two questions (leading devoted-only or close on known roads and devoted anywhere; whether crossing into devoted needs a milestone). |
| `items` | **Itemization** (Jeff, 2026-10-14): owns what items are and how they're held, carried, found and used; coordinates with art-kit (every item has an art slot and grey box; icons rendered from the model), companions (carry, use, talk about items), enemy-ai (monsters carry and drop items; door keys) and perf (item counts within budget). In order, shipping each: (1) item data: one catalog of definitions (id, name, art slot, weight, one/two-handed, tags, effects); every item instance records its history from the start (carriers with time held, up to five notable events), saved with the world; (2) carrying: 4 pack slots and two hands; pick up, drop, swap, throw (toss physics and the item's weight); carried weight adds to mass; chests keep working; (3) icons rendered automatically from each item's art slot, shown in packs and chests; (4) using things: keys open their doors, the bandaging kit works with the bandaging action, consumable buffs (food: heavier, harder to shove; magic items: mind/aura; a hook for social buffs), temporary, never below baseline; (5) weapons as data: hammer, sword, shield, giant axe, spear, dagger, bow with their verb (knockback, block, knockdown, reach, backstab, ranged) and hands, ready for step 6's combat; (6) loot: chests and monsters per map and by builder mode; monsters drop what they carried. Later (go-ahead given, Jeff 2026-10-17): names from long-time holders, absorbing their strength, cursed heirlooms, reforging. Jeff on naming (2026-10-17): "item naming may need a full pass later, but for now Wren's Dagger is sufficient - the idea was that it may strengthen the bond between player and companion - I'm thinking like base weapon drops don't have any special character but a item held and used, perhaps blessed in the temple, or crafted with special materials can "grow", retain kill counts, develop a "bane" preference or a "vengeance" preference": so a growth step after the curse: kill counts kept on the thing, a bane (the kind it has felled most) and a vengeance (the kind that has hurt its holder) preference, a temple blessing (economy's priest) and special materials (lore's, D3) as ways in; plain drops stay plain. Arrows (Jeff, 2026-10-17): "instead of arrows wasting, arrows come in a bundle, one bundle is an archer keeps the bow in one hand the stack in the other, it could be flame arrows, barbed arrows etc. but the stack decays upon being struck with blunt damage and can be repaired by the town fletcher": a bundle is an item held in the other hand from the bow, never used up by shooting, with kinds (plain, flame, barbed...) that set the arrow's effect; it decays when its holder takes blunt blows and a fletcher in town repairs it (economy's trade and job); "enemies could drop arrow bundles, player can swap it out for a fresh one, or decide to keep their better one if and repair it later" (Jeff, 2026-10-17). Priority (Jeff, 2026-10-17: "we need to prioritize bow since archery is already working, it needs play testing"): the archery loop first, end to end for a playtest: draw and loose, the arrow seen in flight, hit and miss, bow clips, bow and bundle art, sounds, monster archers shooting back. A shot never spends an arrow (the bundle rule); "arrows dont harm friendlies, but it might hit and stick out for a little while" (Jeff, 2026-10-17). Heavy weapon wind-up (Jeff, 2026-10-17: "heavy weapon wind up, can be cancelled until swing and then cannot be blocked or cancelled on release"): heavy weapons (giant axe, spear's thrust if items says so) wind up before the blow; during the wind-up a stun, a knock or letting go cancels it; once released the blow lands, not stopped by a guard and not cancelled by anything. Shield bash (Jeff, 2026-10-17: "shield bash is a good addition, approved"): attacking with the shield hand shoves, a step of knockback or a brief stagger, little or no damage. Rules: no damage tiers or rarity treadmill; gear is never consumed, buffs are. Done per step: a short clip or screenshot in play. |
| `economy` | **Resources, stockpile and production** (Jeff, 2026-10-14): owns what the town has, makes and trades, running on the always-on world tick; coordinates with items (economy produces and consumes items, it doesn't define them), maps (territory and regional resources: who holds the mine gets the ore), companions and later town NPCs (who do the work) and perf (the world-tick budget). In order, shipping each: (1) the stockpile: the town's shared store of gold, raw materials (ore, wood, cloth, food) and items; everything players bring home goes in; saved with the world; (2) hauling: loot comes home physically (packs, dragged, carts) and counts when it reaches the stockpile; hooks for carts on tracks later; (3) production: jobs over world ticks (the smith turns ore into weapons and reworks them, the cook turns food into buffs, the carpenter repairs), each needing a worker, materials and time; goods appear in the shop; (4) town growth: town experience from rescues and won runs, spent on upgrades (forge, inn, stronger walls); rescued people unlock their trade; (5) shops and trading, prices from supply and what the town needs; (6) damage and repair: raids damage buildings and take from the stockpile; repairs are production jobs; nothing is lost beyond rebuilding. Later, with the go-ahead: regional resources from held territory, faction trade and upkeep. The fallen come back (Jeff, 2026-10-17: "temple building / priest job in town -- wilds has healer's hut and medicine man job - for now everyone can return"): a temple with a priest in town and a healer's hut with a medicine man in the wilds, the bring-back as a job at either, free for now, everyone included; buildings owns the two building kinds, economy the trades and the job. Farms (Jeff, 2026-10-17: "i wanted to add farms and farmers too - tune with seasons"): a farmer trade and a field building, food grown on the world tick by the season (sowing, growth, harvest, nothing in winter), rain and storms counting; the 30-day balance runs showed the town can't feed itself without one. "thieves could be the answer to balancing the economy" (Jeff, 2026-10-17): the 90-day runs pool gold with whichever maker the town pays most; a thief who lifts from the richest purse and spends or fences what he takes moves it back out, with the risk of being caught. Rules: winning a run earns the big reward, losing never takes what's banked; the economy builds the town, not one player's wealth. Done per step: a short report and a screenshot or log on the world tick. Gold (Jeff, 2026-10-18: "gold economy - makers have more expense - raw material and apprentices - trade with other town. think about establishing trade routes, also introduce luxury goods that traders seek, sell to the rich"): the treasury's drift to its floor (npc's soak) is fixed by expense and trade, not taxes: makers buy raw materials from the store (gatherers paid on delivery; the woodcutter lands first, so wood exists) and pay apprentices; traders carry goods along maps' routes between towns, routes on the world map and raidable; a small luxury catalog the rich and officials buy, dearer with distance. Sheep and wool (Jeff, 2026-10-18: "sheep and wool yes"): cloth comes from wool; a shepherd keeps a flock and shears wool to the store, a weaver makes cloth; creature.sheep slot by the goblin recipe. Rule (Jeff, 2026-10-18): "everything has to be gathered and someone paid to do it, wood cutter, hauler, hunter etc": no material appears from nowhere; every raw thing is gathered by a trade and the gatherer and the hauler are paid per delivery or load. Players (Jeff, 2026-10-18): "players bank goods for gold and if they want they spend gold later to help the town with upgrades and defences - they buy armor weapons and services to sink gold, players lose gold through spend": the store pays a player the delivery price for banked goods; a player's gold goes only by spending (gear and services at the counter, contributions to the town's planned buildings and defences); never lost to death or theft. Taxes (Jeff, 2026-10-18): "taxes can be most higher on luxury goods which the rich prefer then wages are paid by town": the trade tax is by goods class, highest on luxuries, and that tax pays the town's wages. Phase 1 (497 woodcutter, 498 deliveries paid, hauling per load, makers buy materials, player banking paid) measured 2026-10-18: no building in 30 days before; after, the tannery, field and woodshed by day 3 and the inn on day 26, officials unpaid down by half, loans repaid 2 to 31; the treasury still at its floor because the pool is the founding gold, which apprentices, contributions (1b) and the luxury tax (phase 3) address. Apprentices measured (2026-10-18, two 30-day soaks): the smith and the carpenter each took one, 34 daily wages of 2 gold went master to apprentice, the apprenticed haulers ended out of debt (0 gold and a 26 loan before, 11 and none after); makers' purses barely moved; officials still unpaid 249 then 254, so apprentices move gold within the town and don't fill the treasury; the leather price fix (8, from 4: the tanner lost 3 a piece and starved the inn) brings the inn on day 28; scarce hides are the next lever for an earlier inn. Contributions measured (499, 2026-10-18, players giving up to 5 a day): 129 gold given, the inn three days sooner, borrowing halved, the treasury and unpaid officials unchanged; the gold pools with the smith and the carpenter, which is the case for phase 3's luxury tax as the treasury's income. Phase 2 rule (coordinator): a caravan's sale at the market pays the treasury whole (the store bought the goods at delivery), less the merchant's and hauler's trip wages; no luxury tax on the town's own export, the tax is on sales to the rich in town; the market's price falls with what it already bought. Phase 2 landed (504, economy's side, 2026-10-19) and measured over 30 days: 21 caravans, mostly food and wood, none raided, 112 gold fetched (21 in wages, 86 to the treasury), officials unpaid 254 → 236, the treasury still at its floor because the new gold goes straight to what the town owes; the inn didn't stand because the tanner hits his loan cap and the town can't pay him for leather. Phase 3 next (design first): a small luxury catalog brought by the caravans' buy share and dearer with distance, bought by the rich (purses above a line) through the store so the luxury tax lands in the treasury. Design approved 2026-10-19: three luxury items (wine and spice imported from the castle market by the caravans' buy share, dearer with distance; a silver ring the smith makes from ore where a smith works), the town's merchant (Snagfin) buying imports at landed cost and selling to the rich with the margin taxed at 0.5 into the treasury, the rich a purse above 60 with a daily chance by wealth and never on a loan, a maker never buying their own make, wine and spice consumed and the ring kept, a card fact and standing through the trade note; measured against a fresh baseline with the tax gathered and whether the tanner is paid and the inn stands. First soak (2026-10-19): nothing made, imported or sold, the treasury and unpaid officials unchanged, because Held has no ore for the ring and the merchant has no gold to stock imports while the treasury sits at its floor, the rich holding about 290 idle gold. Amended (coordinator): a commission, the rich resident ordering a luxury through the merchant and paying the landed cost up front into the treasury as the order, the caravan fetching it, the merchant paid the rest of his price on return with the 0.5 tax on his margin to the treasury; no new credit; ore reaching the smith waits for the work-site step. Landed (7ac70753) and measured over 30 days: 12 commissions, all imports; the rich paid 180 up front (out through the caravan's sale) and 132 on delivery (48 luxury tax to the treasury, 84 to Snagfin, who cleared his debt); officials unpaid 235 → 204; the tanner paid 28 times and the inn standing on day 23 with the loan cap untouched. Sheep and wool landed (500, c13c001e, 2026-10-19): a pasture once the field is farmed, the shepherd after it, a flock of 6 lambing toward 12 a pasture level, sheared spring and autumn for 2 wool a sheep, a loom at 6 wool and the weaver after it (2 wool to 3 cloth); in the soak the pasture stood on day 23 behind the inn, the shepherd sheared 12 wool, the weaver came on day 29. Drawn sheep in the pasture are a later step (the flock count on the wire, pc-character and terrain). Work-site rule approved (design 2026-10-19): sites by trade (woodcutter: woodlot, hunter: hunting_ground, farmer: farm); no site: today's yield; a held site: the yield with the worker away for the round trip on the slow tick, the hunter at the site; held by raiders or contested: no yield, one line, the town uneasy, back when retaken; unreachable: no yield. Landed as 521 (1ceaf40f) and measured: a woodlot 30 cells out taken by raiders on day 15 took the wood from 37 a day to 0 with the town uneasy; reachability is builder's authoring check, not a runtime test. Build order landed as 522 (83bf967c): after two days stalled on materials the town orders the next build it can (the pasture on day 8 instead of 23), but more makers spread the treasury's floor thinner and the inn missed the month; amended (coordinator): the treasury pays first those owed for what the head build lacks (changed nothing: the tanner's trade doesn't pay his keep, about 1.4 gold a leather against 3 a day, so he hits his loan cap by day 7); amended again (8c72399b): while the head build lacks a raw its maker makes it on the town's account, the cook's meals' mechanism, so the inn stood on day 16. Next for economy (coordinator, 2026-10-19, design first): a livings audit, income and keep a day per trade from the baseline soak, and a proposal on the income side (buy prices from inputs plus a living margin, or a wage floor for a trade the town needs) for the trades that don't clear their keep. The audit (2026-10-19, gold a day): the smith (18.5 in services) and carpenter (15.2) and the farmer and woodcutter clear their keep; the hunter barely; the cook, merchant, tanner, weaver, shepherd, haulers and every official don't, the officials never paid because the treasury never rises above its floor, and the town's money ends with the smith and carpenter (about 34 a day in tool and mending fees, taxed 0.1). Rulings (coordinator): the store buys a made raw at no less than its inputs plus a making margin of 3 a job; a wage floor of 3 a day for the shepherd, hauler, tanner, weaver and cook, topped up by the treasury or owed; and the service tax by the earner's purse, 0.1 below the rich line and 0.3 above it, so the smith and carpenter carry the wages as Jeff's "the rich ... then wages are paid by town" says; Jeff's to veto. Landed as 528 (ec0c596b) and measured: the tanner, weaver, cook and shepherd now clear or nearly clear their keep, the smith and carpenter still clear easily under the higher tax, but the floor (126 of 258 paid) and the officials (never paid, unpaid 229) draw on the same treasury; amended (coordinator): officials' wages paid first among the town's debts and the shortfall owed rather than lost, with the officials' wage at 4 a day instead of 12 (three soaks: paying 12 a day first froze the town, since it was four times their keep and more than the treasury takes in; at 4 a day, near their keep, they were paid in full and the inn still stood on day 16; Jeff's number to move); raising the rich's tax to 0.5 is Jeff's line. Standing fact: with today's inflows the treasury can't pay the floor, the officials and the makers at once, so the next gold comes from trade's volume or the rich. |
| `lore` | **World, factions and story** (Jeff, 2026-10-14): owns the world's facts (history, places, factions, peoples, materials, names), consistent and structured so others build on them; coordinates with maps (regions, factions, territory data), economy (materials and origins), companions (what characters know and how they speak) and builder (what places look like). Parts: the world primer (docs/lore/primer.md; tone grim, fated, loyal, *Gísla saga* as reference; from Jeff's and Dire's ideas, big things asked, not invented); factions (the goblins, the Blood Rot clan and others: who they are, what they want and hold, how they fight and treat captives, how they relate to the town), with names and facts maps' faction data can use; regions and materials (each region's character and resources, e.g. ordinary iron near town, blighted ore in the Blood Rot lands, and what each does when worked); who knows what (common knowledge, rumour, secret, so companions only say what their character would know); names and a style guide. Rules: lore states facts, never scripted lines; structured entries (names, relations, tags) beside the prose so code can read them; new or changed lore reported to main, with anything needing Jeff's decision flagged. Agreed means Jeff said it, word for word (Jeff, 2026-10-17: "Dire is not a dev right now, he's a play tester"): Jeff's answers flip facts to agreed; Dire's ideas are welcome but Dire confirms nothing. |

| `environment` | **Sky, day-night, weather and ambient light** (Jeff, 2026-10-14): owns what the sky does and how the world is lit overall; torches, fires and lanterns stay part of the sim and maps, seen against its global light. Coordinates with terrain (ground and scatter visuals), enemy-ai and companions (sight and hearing use light, fog, rain and wind), sound (ambience follows time and weather), economy (town schedules) and perf (frame and tick budget). In order, shipping each: (1) day-night on the world clock: a sun rising east and setting west, a moon at night; light colour, intensity, sky and fog through dawn, day, dusk and night (warm gold, neutral, warm again, cool blue moonlight); default day 90 real minutes, configurable, pausing with the server; stable, non-shimmering shadows at the game camera; (2) daylight in the sim: a zone-wide daylight term in the light field, outdoors only (roofed areas, interiors and caves stay as dark as their own lights make them), so sight-by-light, sneaking and NPC routines follow the hour; (3) ambient lighting: one setting per zone type (outdoors, indoors, cave, dungeon), dark enough that torches and lanterns matter, never unplayably black; (4) weather: clear, overcast, rain, fog, wind, storms, changing on the world clock with gradual transitions, chosen per region by a simple climate (the Blood Rot lands foggier); visuals (rain, fog, cloud shadow, wind in trees and fire) and play (fog and rain shorten sight, rain and wind mask sound, wind bends fire and smoke). Later (go-ahead given, Jeff 2026-10-17): seasons (light, colour, snow); weather that affects the town (storms damage roofs, rain fills wells). Done per step: a short clip (a compressed full day on the castle map; a fog bank rolling in), and the frame budget holds at 256². |
| `ui` | **Interface and menus** (Jeff, 2026-10-17): "You own how the interface looks, feels and fits together; other trees still own what their screens *do*. Coordinate through main with items (packs, chests, tooltips), economy (shops, commissions), companions and npc (conversations, the talk log), builder (its tools), environment (time of day, weather cues) and sound (UI sounds). Principles: as diegetic as possible — no health, stamina or mana bars; show state through bodies, light and sound, with screens only where the game needs them. Readable at a glance, consistent everywhere, scalable for different screens. Ship in this order: 1. Audit and style: screenshot every current screen and panel, list inconsistencies, and write a short UI style guide (fonts, colours, spacing, panel style) matching the art style; one shared theme all screens use. 2. Core screens: main menu, options (graphics, audio sliders, controls, the body choice), pause, and a proper connect/join flow with server status and update progress. 3. In-game panels: packs, chests and hands as one consistent inventory view, with item tooltips that show history and names ("Wren's dagger"); the talk log and party chat; the compass and HUD hints. 4. Conversations: a panel for talking with townsfolk — commissions, shops, rumours — that shows who's speaking and what they're offering, without hiding the world. 5. The map: a world map of regions and places, with what's known, who holds what, and where your party is. 6. Accessibility: UI scale, text size, colour-blind-safe colours, rebindable keys. Done when each step ships with before/after screenshots, and every screen uses the shared theme." Done (2026-10-17): all six steps on main (401, 418, 427, 436, 441, 445, 453 with the Keys pass), before/after shots in docs/ui/shots. Open for Jeff: the HP number (what shows hurt instead), the main menu beyond Play, Options, Quit. Next: panel frame costs for perf's regression hunt, the bow's draw read diegetically, the merchant's counter when Snagfin lands. |

| `physics` | **The sim's physics** (Jeff, 2026-10-22: "i think we need a physics work-tree to make sure that combat doesn't have to figure out the whole games physics"): owns how bodies move and collide in the sim, so combat expresses its moves as physics events and physics resolves them. Parts: mass, velocity and force on every body (players, companions, monsters, animals, thrown things, arrows); pushback and knockback from a blow by the masses and the angle of contact; momentum (moving into a blow adds force); collision damage and what a body does when it meets a wall, another body or a drop (the push stopping, a stagger, a fall; falls keep their damage); bodies launched and thrown (the staff's grab and throw, a shield bash's shove, a charge down a line knocking back what's in the path), with the sim's cells as the truth and the client interpolating; the ground's effect on movement (slopes, tall grass, fords, ice later) in one place; stamina as a physical cost if combat asks for it there; the arrow's flight as a body (after the archery refinement lands). Rules: the server resolves every collision (no client physics that decides outcomes); one shared model, no per-kind special cases where mass can decide (the doc's "let the sim decide"); every rule has a headless test with numbers (a light body knocked farther than a heavy one by the same blow); perf measures the step's cost before and after; designs to the coordinator first, with combat as the first customer and enemy-ai for monsters' use of the same moves. First: read docs/combat_design.md and the sim's movement and blow code, write the model in a page (what exists: force, mass, pushback, collision damage; what's missing), and design the first step: pushback and knockback by mass and angle, with a test and a clip. Model written 2026-10-22 (C:/dev/review/physics/model.md): mass, strength, a clamped force, one kind-blind push with walls, doors, ledges, drops, water, fire and body chains, impact as damage, a toss, a bash, a brace; missing: velocity and momentum, push at any angle, mass counted honestly (two clamps flatten it), body-on-body as a collision, launching and throwing. Step 1 approved (C:/dev/review/physics/step1_design.md): one apply_impulse, velocity = impulse / mass with no clamps, the attacker's momentum added along the blow, the slide along any angle traced like a shot losing a fixed speed per cell (a 10-cell sanity cap), a wall hit's impact by half m u squared, body on body a 1-D collision with restitution 0.2 replacing the budget and the chain, the brace the charger's own momentum turned back; calibrated to today's feel (the hammer drives a goblin 4 cells, a fist an imp 2); combat decides who, when, damage and the stagger and gets back what moved, how far, what stopped it; physics_test with numbers; perf measures. 592 landed 2026-10-22 (3aa1e297, protocol unchanged). Step 2 approved (C:/dev/review/physics/step2_design.md): World.lunge (the body really steps a cell a tick so its momentum counts; without "through" (the shield bash) it stops at the first body and its blow lands with that momentum; with "through" (the spear's charge) each body met is a collision by mass, a goblin going 2 cells, an ogre stopping it dead with no special case); World.launch for the staff's grab and throw (combat checks the mind cost against the mass) and the heavy shot (the pin a body stopped against a wall with speed left; the existing archery untouched); thrown items move what they hit (impulse = weight × 2), retiring the last push outside 592; one ground_drag shared by walking and slides (slides through a ford, grass or uphill shorter); a guard keeping part of a blow's push on combat's ask; in that order, each landing separately with tests that state numbers. Approved 2026-10-22 night, 607 amended (combat's ask): a lunge's or rush's cells take ticks × ground_drag of the cell entered, the body's speed in that cell slowed so a body, a braced spear or a collision in a ford is met at that speed, its own drive kept so it picks up on open ground (the ground never spends a charger's drive, since it drives itself and isn't sliding); open ground drag 1 so every test stands; physics_test: a rush across a ford 2× the ticks, a 120 kg charge in a ford pushing half as hard; a draugr charge through a ford easier to stop and disarm (combat re-runs its test and clip); server only, no wire change, after the morning cut. |

| `(camera)` | **The camera, two asks** (Jeff, 2026-10-22 night: "couple camera things.. one i think archers should be able to zoom out further.. most animals end up being just off screen. two and bigger... what would you think about trying to drop the camera angle so we can see the sky and the horizon"): (1) ui builds, for a near cut: an aim lead while a bow is held (the view slides off the player toward the cursor as the middle-drag pan does, back when the bow lowers; nothing beyond World.NEAR_TILES, 238) and one more wheel notch out with a bow in hand if perf says NEAR_TILES takes the reach; a sheet at the longest range step. (2) A trial, not a landing: the camera is orthographic at pitch 50, and a lower ortho pitch shows more ground but no horizon, so the trial is a perspective camera at about 30 degrees with a sky and fog to the horizon, dungeons keeping the top-down pitch: ui's sheet (town square from the road, the wilds road to the hills, the castle from the gate; noon and dusk; ortho 50, ortho 35, perspective 30) from a throwaway scene in build/, with what breaks noted (far picking, the near/far wall rule, nameplates, the telegraph decal, the zone's edge in view, entities beyond NEAR_TILES standing still, the ridge rule) and perf's frame cost; terrain and environment own the horizon's look if Jeff picks it; the coordinator shows Jeff and he picks. Perf (same night): no extra wheel notch at NEAR_TILES 22 (one notch fits 16:9 but not 21:9, 22.4 and 25.1 peeking), so the aim lead alone, within about 4 cells; the notch needs NEAR_TILES about 25, estimated under 1 ms a tick with 16 players (238's 22→28 was +1.4 ms), benched if Jeff asks; the trial scene reports its own frame times (render CPU/GPU median and p95) in a bench slot; the horizon's terrain costs draw only (the client builds the whole zone's ground already), but entities beyond 22 cells think one tick in four, idle past 30, freeze past 60, so a horizon view fades entities out beyond about 22 cells or widens the tiers at a server cost. Perf's trial numbers (same night, 1600x900, noon, square/road/gate, three alternating rounds of 300 frames, alone in the bench slot): the perspective camera at 30 degrees costs about +0.1 to +0.5 ms render CPU and up to +0.3 ms GPU a frame against today's ortho at 50 (square CPU 0.85-0.92 against 0.35, GPU 3.07-3.09 against 2.76-2.81; road CPU 0.62-0.70 against 0.30-0.34, GPU about 2.7 against 2.9; gate CPU 0.85-0.91 against 0.75-0.79, GPU 2.43-2.68 against 1.95-2.23); ortho at 35 is within noise of today; so the horizon's ground and scatter cost under 1 ms a frame and the pick is a design call, not a perf one. Two caveats: light shadows are switched on by the ortho camera's reach (Client3D._update_shadows), so a real perspective camera needs that rule redone or far lights lose their shadows, and dusk with lanterns costs more than noon; the process time sits on vsync at 16.6 ms, and the square's p95 of about 95 ms is the host's town tick, not the camera. The full table is with release. Ui's trial sheets (2026-10-22 night, C:/dev/review/ui/camera_trial: camera_trial_noon.jpg, camera_trial_dusk.jpg, camera_trial_notes.md; rows town square, wilds road, forest edge on the wilds and the hills, castle gate; columns ortho 50 as today, ortho 35, perspective 30 at half distance with fog; a throwaway scene in build/, nothing committed): ortho 35 reads like today with a longer look ahead and costs nothing; perspective 30 is dramatic at the castle and the hills' edge and is a real build (the land's hard end needs a far ridge or heavier fog, far entities and nameplates in view with the fade beyond 22 cells, far picking, the near-wall cut as a slab, the shadow reach rule, the zone's edge and what lies under the land in view, the ridge rise larger; dungeons stay top-down). Amended (coordinator, 2026-10-23, terrain's question): the deferred outline rebuild is a full rebuild and freezes the local player 1 to 3.5 s wherever they stand, so it never runs mid-visit; the ring's ground and the edge curve rebuild only on the next zone load, and a felled edge cell shows its stump on the forest floor until then; stage changes, fells and regrowth still at once. If the ring must move while people watch, that is an incremental rebuild for perf later, never a scheduled full one. Jeff (13:xx, after playing v0.1.169's low camera): "the camera view add so much - direction, context, the hills are readable the landmarks visible. amazing. but the game is not optimized. movement was glitchy and fighting were much more challenging... i think it's well worth the change. what are the downsides?": the low camera (perspective 22) is the game's direction; it stays a toggle until the glitches and the fight readability are fixed, then becomes the default (the ortho view kept as the Off setting). Routed: perf measures on/off in play and finds the movement glitch (frame hitching vs bodies or the player snapping under perspective interpolation); ui and combat the fight readability at the low angle (targets behind bodies and walls, foreshortened telegraph and arc, click hit areas, the near-wall cut, enemies appearing at the 22-tile fade); terrain the land's edge and the far ground; environment the fog planes, cloud decal and rain box sized for the ortho footprint. Terrain's dais design approved (indoors only, an edge one height level apart gets dais.edge, dais.steps where a stair climbs onto it, stair cells drawn flat, the cliff fallback, view_test on an in-memory 12x4 dais). Companions next: 581 step 1, 608's search, SLEEP on the wire. Environment's 623 amended approved (13:xx, after 608 amended, client only, ortho unchanged): the weather's share of fog density scaled by CAMERA_DISTANCE / camera_distance() under the low camera (the aim reads as in ortho, storms hide the hills; today's haze reads thinner near, not thicker); ground fog planes faded to their rims, 3x wider and centred ahead; the rain/snow box stretched toward the view (about 34 by 70, drops capped at 1.5x); the cloud decal 3 to 5 tiles with Godot's distance fade; the sky's horizon colour equal to the fog colour under weather; shadow distance 150 measured by perf against 70 before any change; day_night_test and the weather view's tests; a sheet ortho against low in clear, fog, rain and cloud after the all-clear. Perf found 623's bug (13:xx): in Client3D._sync_puppets the lines entity.set_process(true) and entity._display_caught_up() that belong to `if _far.has(id):` sit under `if _low:`; low camera on, every drawn body snaps to its tile every frame (Jeff's glitchy movement, the harder fights; knockbacks and lunges teleport); off, a body that went beyond reach is never restarted and stands frozen (in v0.1.169 for everyone). Fix: the two lines back under _far; ui lands it on main first, release cuts a client-only hotfix from the v0.1.169 tag plus the cherry-pick (main carries revision 34), no deploy. Perf's camera measurements after the fix. Ui's fight-readability plan ruled (13:xx): after the hotfix, each its own client-only landing: (1) the fade lines run to every hostile within a few tiles during a fight and a building the camera looks past to the player gets its near side cut; (2) pick slack in screen pixels turned to ground units by the body's distance, nearest along the ray wins; (4) puppets ease in and out at the reach edge over the wall-fade time; (5) nameplates scaled by distance with a floor, depth-sorted, beyond a few tiles only the engaged foe's and the party's. (3) a low translucent band standing on the telegraph's and arc's rims waits until Jeff plays the fixed build. No x-ray silhouette (the sim's sight rule decides); no longer near reach for now. Finish line: the low camera becomes the default (ortho the Off setting) when Jeff says a fight reads after 1, 2, 4, 5, perf shows no hitch, and environment's seams and terrain's edge are in. Release: only main is released and main is on revision 34, so the hotfix is a full v0.1.170 from main with the fix first (a fix Jeff has seen, the cadence rule), client first, a deploy, regenerate barrow_deep and barrow_lair; it carries the archery (flight, cone), armour step 1 and combat's side, the card page, stacking's interface, the cues, the loyalty tiers whole, the focus words. Combat's numbers (ortho 50 against low 22: a cell's depth 0.31x, ground hidden behind a 1.5 m body 1.3 to 3.7 cells, behind the draugr 4.5): the telegraph is the unfair loss (the spin's 2-cell ring a sliver behind his own body), so the ruling on 3 is reversed: after the hotfix and 1, 2, 4, 5, ui builds (a) the telegraph stood up, a low curtain or posts about 0.4 m on its rim drawn after bodies with no depth test, fill alpha by pitch; (c) a ring at the feet of World.stand_target instead of the cell; (d) the cone wedge's edges lifted about 0.1 m, the fill raised below 30 degrees; (b) a see-through outline only for a foe the sim already sent that a nearer body hides on screen or inside an active telegraph, never through walls or buildings; the sim the same at either camera. Jeff (2026-10-23, 13:40, playing v0.1.169): "I can't bandage Pip, Pip seems lazy at bandaging. Check play test dialogue. Just says \"fine\". Bandages should have a ruffling sound and a kneeling next to the target animation. If target moves it interrupts." What the code says: a bandage (World.try_bandage) wants both calm (30 ticks since either was hurt, no live monster within 6 cells), the patient hurt and not mending, the bandager beside the patient with a kit in a slot; when any of that fails the player's B-click or E-hold does nothing at all on screen, and the companion's errand ends with a reason only the model sees ("it was not safe to bandage"), which Pip then says as "fine"; sfx.bandage has three takes in the store, marked "not played yet"; nothing kneels; the patient moving breaks the bandage only when it leaves melee reach, not on any step. Routed: companions (lead: a plain refusal in Pip's own words with the reason, the player's refusal shown too, the bandage broken off on the patient's first step, a journal grep through release for Pip's bandage lines, no ids), sound (play sfx.bandage over the bandaging, a cloth ruffle, with the done and interrupted cues), pc-character (a kneel beside the patient for the bandager, both player and companion, dropped on interruption). A build, not a pick; no folder file. pc-character's kneel design approved (2026-10-23, 13:55): World.bandage_changed is server-only and only the patient's mending replicates, so a joined client can't tell who kneels; one wire field GridEntity.tending (the patient's name while bandaging, "" otherwise; set in try_bandage, cleared on done and every interrupted path; SUBCLASS_REPLICATED on Player and Companion, a revision line, after the v0.1.170 cut), a "bandage" ACTIVITIES entry (Fixing_Kneeling looped) through work_of whenever tending is set, the body turned to the patient and leaned about 0.3 m toward it, a gait always winning over the kneel, view_test and items_test. Sound drives sfx.bandage from the same field; companions clears it on the patient's-step break and agrees the landing order with pc-character. Companions' design approved (14:05): the root of "fine" is 616's stock word, the one Pip always picks by name hash, said when her voice hasn't answered within 4 s of an order; the bandage then fails (usually not calm) with the reason only the model sees. Build: refusals from data at once (companion_traits bandage_says: "Not here. Not with them this close.", "Give it a breath. We're still bleeding from that.", "I've nothing to bandage with.", "There's nothing to bandage." / "I'm not hurt.", "Hold still, or I can't bind it.", "It's bound already. Give it time."), checked when the order is read, the voice told as narration only; the player's refused B-click or E-hold answered by World.bandage_refusal as a "Server" party line to that player ("You can't bandage Pip: a monster is near." and the like), and the break-off told the same way; the patient's first step off their cell breaks it off (the patient's tile kept from the start); release's grep of today's lines, counts and wording only. Jeff (2026-10-23, 15:05, play-test notes on v0.1.169): "no way to give gold to draugr, draugr no longer walks but has a long reach. draugr door closes to quickly companion can't get in. draugr abilities should be in a smooth pattern like a cone similar to archery, not blocky tiles. not enough skeletons in the ossuary and the ones that are are lying down or stuck in their niches. The mortally wounded screen doesn't go gray like it was meant to." What the code says and where each went: (1) the draugr's gold goes only through a typed line in earshot (Main._monster_said: ask for wares or work, then say an amount or yes); E on him does nothing (a tap opens talk on a Resident only) and there is no give or barter on him: npc (the offer contract) and ui (E on a talking monster opens the talk window with his deals as buttons from the purse). (2) Seated he fights nobody unless struck and keeps_room is on; the spin (603, every 60 ticks) reaches past his cell: enemy-ai diagnoses with physics whether he should come off the throne when a pact or a blow starts him, and why he stands. (3) The Hall shuts on the first living player in the lair (_tend_hall), the companion behind shut out: enemy-ai with companions; ruling: the door waits for the player's companion while she is within a few cells of it and coming, up to about 5 s. (4) The telegraphs as smooth cones and arcs, not cell squares, the sim still in cells: Jeff's direction, to combat with ui, folded into combat's (a) standing telegraph. (5) barrow_deep spawns 6 skeletons; once the draugr is laid to rest, World.dead_rest lays every skeleton of his side still for his rest (580), which is what "lying down" looks like; niche-placed ones may not path out: dungeon (counts, niche cells with a way out) and enemy-ai (whether the rest should empty the ossuary); release greps today's journal for laid-to-rest and dead_rest lines, counts and times only. (6) _return_place takes the nearest manned place of any kind and skips a hut whose care the player lacks, so a nearer manned temple wins over Hob's hut even in his care, and a hut unmanned (Hob away or asleep) falls through: npc with economy; ruling: in care, the carer's hut first when manned, else the nearest. (7) Aldous's 8 gold is return.offering (546), waived for the draugr's blessed: by design, said to Jeff; the real fault is (6). (8) The bleed-out scream is sfx.death_player (kenney-impact death_player_1-4) on Client3D._fall, one sound for a sudden death and a bleed-out: sound (a last breath for the bleed-out, the scream kept or changed for a sudden death; a sheet of takes for Jeff to hear). Physics found a second cause (22:05), one that shows only with two zones awake, which the box always has: World.step runs _step_zone per awake zone with tick swapped per zone, but _rushers (604), _boss_moves and _axes (603) are global dictionaries tended under every awake zone's step with no zone check (the other global tenders skip by.zone != zone.name), so a spin or throw can land during the overworld's step against its occupancy, and a charge's rush sets next_move_tick from the overworld's tick, hours ahead of the lair's: he never steps again while _try_hit still strikes whoever comes beside him. Approved as 604 amended: _tend_rushes skips a rusher of another zone; combat the same in _tend_boss_moves, _tend_charge and _tend_disarmed; a two-zone headless test; server only; release greps the zone wake lines and his rush cells during the fight. Economy's rise-and-fall design approved (19:07 PDT) with six amendments: the stage is "settlement" (Jeff's word), STAGES = [settlement, hamlet, village, town, city]; one kind, buildings' "camp" (S plot 5x5 or 6x6, open all round, tents and a fire ring as composer parts, built by anyone, economy's cost), not tent + fire_pit; livestock reuses building.fold at stage village by the carpenter, no pen or byre; economy says who takes a settlement's upkeep (no carpenter lives there); before landing, the default world (the wilds town as the map has it) runs 30 sim days with the fall rules and upkeep on and must hold (strain 0, nothing unkept), drafts tuned if not, never the rule disabled, the checklist carrying a rules.decline tune line; the rest built as drafted: a hamlet needs 4 residents, a farm or fold and wood 20, a village the temple, inn, forge and a fold; strain +1 a day for residents under the stage's count, over half hungry, or officials unpaid, -1 on a clean day, a fall of one stage at 10 (never below settlement), +3 at once for a raid's captives, the needs met again before a rise; upkeep wood 1 a week per level from the store by the carpenter's turn, a building unkept 14 days (upkeep short, DAMAGED unrepaired, or no one of its trade) goes DISREPAIR (out of use, a level a season) then RUIN at level 0 (no building, the record kept a season), the furthest from the stockpile first, repair lifting DISREPAIR at a cost by the levels lost, a ruin rebuilt only as a new build, no gold lost; Towns emits ruined(town, kind, cells) and Main hands the cells to Clearing.unbuild(zone, cells, day) at stage stump (terrain's call; fallow fields and old tent ground the same); a fresh world with the map's "start": "tents" seeds no buildings, a saved map seeds as today, nothing falls on load, the box's town untouched. Order: (a) economy's sim with the sheet's strain line (wire, under 35), (b) terrain's unbuild, (c) buildings' camp kind and one generic disrepair/ruin treatment (roof gaps, leaning walls, dark door, weeds; walls cut to a stub, rubble, undergrowth), (d) maps' settlement layout (camp plots round the stockpile, reserved plots for inn, temple, forge, farm and fold, plot_for reading the reserved list first, edge plots furthest out so ruin runs in reverse of growth) and the start flag; ui's sheet line; lore's words optional. Economy asks release for the number; each part lands as "N, <tree>'s side"; buildings does not edit STAGES (economy's (a) does). Routed to physics (Fields), perf if the cause is zone teardown: a repro test first, then the fix, maps told to restore its test order.   Physics found the cause (05:55 PDT): World.despawn only removed a body from the zone World was in, so despawning a body in another zone freed it but left it in its own zone's entities and on its cell while the current zone's occupancy at that cell was erased instead; everything walking that zone later hit the freed body (Fields.any_carried_heat via _nearest_free, _step_zone). The box's disconnect, travel and raid paths enter the body's zone first, so it mostly escaped; any other cross-zone despawn (a companion or resident path) would leave the same ghost. Fix: despawn enters the entity's own zone when it differs, then returns (8 lines in sim/world.gd, no guards in Fields); zone_test _test_bounce_back right after _test_restart (2 FAIL and 2 SCRIPT ERRORs before, clean after); server sim only, no wire, no class_name. Dungeon (approved 2026-10-22 late): the barrow's three levels were arrangements already (templates holding fixed rooms as anchors, the generator laying every passage and tomb), so the single tiles were the generator's and the fix is there: the ring that narrowed the way to a tomb's door goes (the way five wide to a walled tomb, its door the one cell), a widen pass after the carving on the deep caverns' blind ways and join-ups (every single-width run gets a cell beside it; the crooked blind ways become two-wide stubs, no longer dead ends), dwarven corridors two wide into plain rooms; Level.narrow_runs checks every generated style and layer (labyrinth_test) and every regenerating template (level_test); a grave alcove a cell deep stays, a dead end nobody passes through; the first hall's bays go two rows tall, the niche rows to 2,4,6,8,12,14,16 and the hidden niche to (51,16) with its alcove at (52,16) (terrain and art-kit told; enemy-ai's anchors stay); decision 612, on main 2026-10-22 night as 5b52abd5 after 608 the hidden door (971fb85b) and terrain's niche draw (1cfa8c08), riding the morning cut; release runs regenerate now for barrow, barrow_deep and barrow_lair after that cut's deploy. | `combat` | **Combat feel** (Jeff, 2026-10-22: "today is going to be about combat feel and archery refinement" and "we also need a combat work-tree"): owns how fighting feels for the player, as opposed to what monsters decide (enemy-ai), how bodies move (pc-character), what weapons are (items) or what the screen shows (ui). Parts: the player's melee verbs and their timing (wind-up, strike, recovery, how a swing lands on a moving target), hit feedback (hit-stop, knockback, a stagger, the camera's nudge, the blow's sound cue with sound), blocking, dodging and the cost of them, the damage model shared by players, companions and monsters (what a blow takes, where armour and the focus come in), the shot in the sim (the archery change of 2026-10-22 lands with items and enemy-ai, then combat owns World.shoot and the ranged verbs), and the fights' pace (how long a fight with a brute or a wolf should take at a given focus). Rules: the sim decides every hit (no client-side hits); every change is felt in a play clip before it lands (a short clip beside a before, in C:/dev/review/combat/); designs to the coordinator first, numbers as drafts for Jeff; no new monsters or weapons as content, those are enemy-ai's and items' on Jeff's ask. First: an audit of today's melee from a play clip (the sword, the axe, the fists against a brute and a wolf) and a design for the first feel pass; then the shot's handover. Jeff's brief (2026-10-22, docs/combat_design.md, landed by combat): the same click gives different outcomes by angle, timing, momentum, what's held and what's around (Dire's critique of click-to-adventure); moves are physics events on mass and velocity; no gesture casting; the mouse is for combat, the keyboard for the rest; the input map [decided]: left click moves (attack on an enemy click proposed), a stand-and-swing key with left click (Shift proposed), Ctrl plus click the weapon's signature move, right click defends (block or parry by what's held), B puts a bandage on the cursor; swings cost stamina by weapon weight, rapid spinning swings drain it, an exhausted state; the first three kits sword and shield (stamina), staff (mind), bow (focus, added alongside the existing archery, never changing it); later the spear, hammer and giant axe; build order: input plumbing, stamina, sword and shield, the bow's additions, the staff, the feel pass. Open with Jeff: the bow's right button (today the draw, in the doc defend and backstep). Jeff's answer (2026-10-22): "left mouse hold and release to fire could work, right mouse to back-step": the bow's draw moves to the left button held, release fires (the archery refinement's hold steps the range the same way), the right button is the backstep; defend stays the right button for the other kits. Combat's audit (2026-10-22, C:/dev/review/combat/step1_audit_and_input.md): the sword and fists fight identically (a brute in 2.5 s over 5 blows), the giant axe 5.4–6.3 s with wind-ups and knockdowns, attacks cost no stamina today, nothing but the axe moves the 70 kg brute. Step 1 approved: Shift+click stand-and-swing at the cursor point, Ctrl+click the held weapon's signature from items' data (the bash first, moved off the left click), the right button defend (the shield aimed at the cursor; the bow untouched pending Jeff; an empty hand keeps grab and toss), B a bandage on the cursor (a generic cursor-item mode; E-hold kept beside it until Jeff has played it), builder mode moved to K; "swing" gains a point, new "signature" and "apply_item" commands, protocol 31; the physics split agreed (damage, timing, guard and stamina combat's; push, momentum and collisions physics'). Step 2 approved (C:/dev/review/combat/step2_stamina.md, drafts in Jeff's folder): a swing costs ceil(3 + 2 × weapon kg × 70 / wielder mass) (fists 3, sword 6, hammer 9, giant axe 13), a whiff 1.25×, a wind-up paying as it begins, today's brute fight about 30 of 100; winded at 0 lifting at 25 (1.5× step ticks, half damage, the push left to physics' one stamina fade so it isn't counted twice, no signature, regen delay 20, pushed as half mass; 597); numbers in combat's levels/combat.json; players and companions, a per-row monster switch off until enemy-ai's design; winded replicated (protocol); ui's bar, pc-character's stance and breath. Boss fights (Jeff, 2026-10-22: "combat leads, start with the draugr"; his words: "i think the draugr will have a large battle axe that he drops as loot, he has a telegraphed wind up spin that he stands still, winds up for 1.5 seconds thing a devestating swing, a small red cone on the ground indicates the area,  his stamina drops to 10% after that and slowly builds back up, he might have another move thats a overhead axe throw. he winds up and throws it, you can tell the direction he's going to throw it by a longer narrow cone. after he throws the axe he uses charges to the axe to pick it up, if you are able to interrupt the charge he is disarmed to only using fists."): combat leads boss-fight mechanics with enemy-ai (the boss's mind) and physics (the moves) as its halves; the draugr first, inside 573 amended's hall: his great axe (items' kind, loot when he's laid to rest), the standing 1.5 s wind-up spin with a small red ground cone (a shared telegraph the server sends and ui draws, designed once for every boss) and a devastating sweep, his stamina to 10% after and building back (the winded rules on his row), the overhead throw along a longer narrow cone as a launched body, the charge to retrieve it, and a disarm to fists if the charge is interrupted; design to the coordinator, numbers as drafts, clips in C:/dev/review/combat/draugr/. Design approved 2026-10-22 (C:/dev/review/combat/draugr/design.md, physics' half draugr_physics.md): the telegraph once for every boss, a shape (cone, line, ring) whose one cell function both draws it and decides the hit, a "telegraph" message (a protocol line), ui's red decal; the 7 kg battle axe dropped on a won pact; the spin a 1.5 s standing wind-up, a 2-cell cone 150° each side with a 60° gap behind, stopped only by a stun, the sweep 2.5× his blow breaking guard and knocking down with physics' fanned push, then 10 stamina for about 5 s of opening (he backs to his dais, the dead covering), full after about 20 s; the throw a 1.2 s wind-up on an 8-cell line, physics' hurl hitting the first body at 2×, a 0.6 s roar, then physics' rush to the axe hittable all the way, mass deciding the interrupt (about three sword hits from the front, a hammer, two bodies or a braced spear; nothing from behind), any stop or the axe taken first disarming him to fists until he walks back to it; his mind enemy-ai's (back off at 10% till 25, the dead in waves at 75/50/25% hp, spin on 2+ foes or two hits in 3 s, throw at 4–8 cells); drafts in Jeff's folder; after the hall and physics' rush, sweep and hurl. Shipped in v0.1.165 as the prototype. Morning-cut tunings (coordinator, 2026-10-22 evening, after Jeff's play test): (1) the draugr's own blow with the axe is a heavy strike with no knockdown, named on his row and read through Items.verb_from, a player's battle axe keeps its knockdown (combat bcde7252, committed; items' a5a55cf3 ships the axe-knocks-down-only-on-spin stopgap in v0.1.167 tonight and combat restores the axe row with items' numbers in the same morning landing); (2) no throw when closing inside 4 cells, throws from 6 or more (enemy-ai aca2f986); (3) his breath 8 stamina every 7 ticks (was 2 every 4), row key "stamina_regen": measured headless under a nonstop sword, 57 s between spins armed and 27 s disarmed (fists cost less; the fist sweep is a 1-cell cone), where the old rate gave no second spin in 3 minutes; both drafts for Jeff in 603 amended. 603 amended on main 2026-10-22 night as 7930422e (the heavy strike, breath 8 every 7 ticks, the players' axe knockdown back, the bound axe) with enemy-ai's walk-back and recall f88c3814, protocol unchanged, needs the morning deploy; the clip C:/dev/review/combat/draugr/draugr_fight.mp4 (the pact, the spin, the throw dodged from 6 cells, the charge stopped, fists, the yield with the axe recalled; the walk-back not shown since the player wins 3 s after the disarm). |
| `maps` | **World map and connectivity.** One graph of every area (nodes) and how they connect (edges): today's point links in each `level.json` plus edge links (`neighbours`: walk off one edge, arrive at the matching edge at the same position). A validation pass: links go both ways where they should, spawn points exist, no area is unreachable. A node map view in the editor (Godot's `GraphEdit`/`GraphNode`): areas as boxes, links as wires, add/remove/retarget links, open an area. Routing between areas over the graph (Godot's `AStar2D`) for the world tick to move groups across areas. Builder pass 4 builds on it. Done (decisions 194–206, with territory: places, factions, holdings, routes that keep off held ground). Sending each player only nearby entities is done (decision 217, Interest: 8-cell chunks within ~36–44 cells); measured at 16 and 24 players (2026-10-18, under 217): 24 walking clients join at 80 KB/s against 454 off, the steady tick 7.7 ms against 31.5; a wilds death no longer floods the map to find the temple (503: about 2 s of stall to 25 ms). Wildlife richness for herds (368). Next (Jeff's lore pages, 2026-10-17, `docs/lore/castle.md`, `blood_rot.md`): the forest level and closed links for Jeff's forest zone; then delve-again places: "The overworld stays consistent. The labyrinths under the castle are the delve-again places: they make new maps fairly often. The tower's inside shifts too", with fixed anchors (castle gate and keep; a gatehouse below and a deepest chamber; the tower's entrance, way down and star chamber) and the rest regenerating on the slow tick; maps designs it, infrastructure not content. "castle stays persistent, its the entrance marker for the shifting halls which are underground" (Jeff, 2026-10-17): the castle level is never regenerated; the halls are a separate level below it (now dungeon's). Next (coordinator, 2026-10-18, for economy's phase 2): the trade route as a thing on the world graph, a route between two held places across areas that a caravan group walks on the slow tick, raidable where it crosses unheld ground near a Blood Rot camp, visible on the world map; maps designs the route and the travel, economy the goods and prices, enemy-ai the raid. Design approved 2026-10-18 (TradeRoutes, modelled on 328's raids): a route between two ends, a place or a market named in level.json ("markets", one entry in the castle at the keep courtyard, the coordinator's placement for Jeff to move), worked out over the open layer, exposed where it crosses unheld ground near a hostile camp, eta and risk for economy's prices, caravans walked on the world tick, saved, raided for food and people only (295) with members as captives, never gold (settled at the market); drawn on the world map as a dashed way with a dot per caravan (protocol bump). Snagfin never goes (471). Landed as 504 (968b6115); the castle market at (16, 9) in the keep is the coordinator's placement until Jeff moves it. Streams, fords and bridges (design approved 2026-10-18, with terrain and environment): water cells with an ordered "streams" path for the flow; a "ford" legend kind that is slowed floor (ford_pace 0.5, a routing cost); a bridge as a building slot standing on water with a ramped deck and railings that stop walkers not sight; validations and level warnings; the editor's ford paint and stream tool; a generator (Streams.lay, usable by dungeon); maps the data, routing and editor, terrain the drawing, art-kit the bridge slot; tests and one stream in levels/sample only until Jeff asks; a ford in spate is environment's later, after a gameplay yes on being cut off by water. Landed as 505 (9c2e980f): companions follow over a bridge; banks must be level (heights are whole levels), ramped bridges wait until wanted; terrain's drawing approved 2026-10-18 (the water mask carries fords and bridged cells so the shore runs through, a flow texture from the path order drives the ripples, fords pale over their footing with a foam band and a splash, bridges as art-kit's deck, rail and end pieces at the banks' height with the water under; sound's cues for the splash and the running water). Next for maps (coordinator, 2026-10-18): the editor's properties panel from the backlog, since markets, streams, fords, bridges and regenerate joined links, starts, chests and places as hand-edited level.json keys.
