| `terrain` | **Visual terrain pass** (Jeff, 2026-10-14). The grid stays the rules; the visuals stop showing it. No sim or map-format changes. Ground: one continuous mesh per zone chunk, terrain types blended in a shader with noise (no tile seams or straight colour borders). Height: smooth slopes between levels, sharp drops only at true ledges, drawn as rocky cliff faces. Water: one surface per body, soft irregular shoreline, slight transparency, gentle motion. Walls: continuous runs with varied height, broken tops, occasional gaps. Off-grid scatter: tufts, stones, roots, flowers (random offset, size, rotation; density by terrain); trees clumped and varied. Done when before/after screenshots of the castle and the wilds from the normal camera show no grid, and 256² stays within the frame budget. Study (Jeff, 2026-10-17): "we might wanna just consider designing a generative method for the trees. something like growing them from saplings in a sim and the crowns don't overlap and grow into a true canopy": a generative tree method, grown rather than meshed, with crowns that compete for space and form a true canopy; terrain leads the study with art-kit, a prototype image and a cost before any commitment. Then (Jeff, 2026-10-17): "i like the pine canopy, what I want to experiment with is a forest zone - while walking through it would be dark because of the canopy overhead, with trees nearly everywhere.. but spread enough to walk around.. with small clearings where sunlight shines through, maybe some rays shining down in a few areas.. this would be the pathway to the elven strong hold.. protected by forest": a forest zone, a demo level like hills, linked from the wilds, dark under a continuous grown canopy with walkable spacing, clearings with sunlight and a few light rays, the way to the elven stronghold (lore: the dark elven builders of the tower); terrain leads with environment (light under the canopy, rays), maps (the level and links), lore and sound. The trees: "the idea for the trees was to build a sim outside the game to model how trees look in a forest.. not necessarily in game." (Jeff, 2026-10-17): terrain's v2 pines are a one-shot procedural model, not a growth sim; terrain builds the real one as an offline grower (years, a light grid, shading, dying branches, self-thinning) that bakes each tree's shape into the level; pines and the forest zone first. The canopy over the player (Jeff, 2026-10-18: "next tree forest map, use transparency that moves with player to see through the tree tops since its a isometric game"): the forest level is the next map; crowns between the camera and the player go transparent in a soft region that follows the player, built on the wall near-fade (188), no cut hole, shadows and the dark under the canopy unchanged; the region covers most of the view (Jeff, 2026-10-18, on the concept sheet: "concept is close, larger circle, probably even most of visible screen"), the canopy a rim at the screen's edge. Drawing for maps' outdoors (2026-10-19): streams, fords and bridges (505, fc63703b), tall grass and the trampled mat (508, 6444599e; the first landing drew nothing in play because the game's first build took another path, caught by a shot from the game itself), rocks and slopes (513, cc761c8a; the view draws no wall round a rock). Next (coordinator, design first): fire that looks like fire for torches, braziers, campfires and hearths, flames, flicker within environment's light rules, faint smoke, the warm pool; with environment and art-kit. Design approved 2026-10-19: a shader flame on three crossed billboards in the palette's warm row with embers and one faint smoke quad (Flames, placed at a slot's flame socket so campfires and forges get fire for free); the torch marker on art-kit's torch post; the flicker on the client's own lights only (two octaves, torches ±8%, fires ±14%, no position jitter), Fields' light steady so bodies never blink; torches fade by day outdoors; lanterns steady; the castle's frame time measured before and after. |

Picks (Jeff, 2026-10-18: "go ahead and apply the candidates and ill veto in game"): a slot with candidates no longer waits for a pick; art-kit applies the candidate art-gen or its own note names, lands it, and Jeff vetoes in game; a veto goes to art-gen as a redo with his words; `docs/art_picks.md` stays the record. |
| `enemy-ai` | **Monster AI pass** (Jeff, 2026-10-14): owns how monsters decide; perf owns how often they think and the tick budget, maps owns territory and routing, companions share one perception layer (what an agent sees, hears and remembers), art-kit owns door states. In order, shipping each: (1) senses: sight by light (seen if lit or glowing; carried lanterns make you visible), sight cones from facing, blocked by walls and closed doors; hearing: fights, doors, footsteps and speech make noise with a radius (speech ~10 cells, ALL CAPS farther, /w barely); (2) alertness: idle/patrol → suspicious → hunting → fighting and back, with a leash so escape is possible; (3) doors: most monsters can't open closed doors, they lose you or break them (brutes faster); (4) temperament (bold/cautious/cowardly) and groups that call for help and flee together; (5) factions: defend what their faction holds, avoid enemy ground unless raiding. Later, with the go-ahead: sieges with waves and lulls, carrying off a fallen companion. Done per step: a short clip shows it working (sneaking past a goblin facing away in the dark; losing a pursuer behind a closed door) and perf's budgets hold. |

Main folder = home base: pulls, plays the latest, small chores, keeps this file and
`CLAUDE.md` current. One Claude Code session per folder.

## The first scenario: the smith

A farmstead near the castle was raided and the goblins took the smith. Fight through the
fields and the overrun castle, find him held in the keep, bring him home.

- **Loot:** weapons and gear in the castle's chests and on its goblins.
- **Rescue:** the smith becomes the first town resident and reworks weapons.
- **Companions:** react to the raid, the captive, and what's found.
- **World primer** (now the `lore` worktree, docs/lore/primer.md): a page on the town, goblins and land so companions talk like they live
  there — to write with Dire. Tone reference: *Gísla saga* (grim, fated, loyal; named
  weapons and grudges).

The wilds town is named Held (Jeff, 2026-10-18: "town named Held").

Tone (Jeff, 2026-10-19: "the graphic tone has become much more muted ... the colors are dull, the graphics not crisp"; then "tone 8 approved, good improvement"): the cause was the art swap of 10-18 (sandstone to grey brick, pure green to olive) and a sky seen clear at noon only 15% of the time; landed or landing: the shadow map at 8192, the soft dirt and soft edges, eight props rebuilt by hand, terrain's ground lift (grass hue toward green, saturation and value up, dirt gentle), art-kit's sandstone wall tile with a warm stone row for the castle's posts and the same grade on the other tiles, environment's sky defaults (weather toward clear with rain kept, less greying under cloud, a fresh world opening clear, 14-day seasons with yields adjusted, autumn and winter halved, the haze capped, the noon look from 12°, cloud patches halved). The well (Jeff, 2026-10-19: "we owe the town well a hand made pass - its auto slop"): rebuilt by hand by art-kit, ahead of the remaining tiles. Shadows (Jeff: "one building had a shadow about 30-40 tiles long"): a shadow capped at about three times its caster's height, the low sun's shadows faded out.

Archery (Jeff, 2026-10-19: "tested archery, its clean but bow holding and firing animation is awkward"): and "arm and bow clipping body is probably one of the more obvious things to fix"): then on v0.1.160: "the bow draw back arm is all wrong it clips through the body then is held as if the arm is behind wrapping behind the head to hold it" (the string arm, not checked by the first pass; fixed next); and "bow arm is too long, bow is held out too far away from body too" (the bow hand's reach measured against the arm's rest length per build; the carried bow brought in to the thigh). Jeff (2026-10-21, v0.1.162): "bow mechanic looking nice, a few fixes. running with walking carrying arrow bundle and bow at side needs to be bow swinging with arm, arrows should just be a quiver on the back and right arm swinging while running like normal. bow string doesn't bend, it needs real physics": 462 amended again (pc-character, art-kit's quiver slot): the bow swings with the left arm on the run and walk, the right arm free, no bundle in hand, the arrows a quiver on the back; the string's middle follows the draw hand through the draw and springs back on release with a short damped settle. Landed eb2fdda3 (v0.1.163) with art-kit's quiver 0732647a. Jeff (2026-10-21 evening): "bow3 is perfect, the quiver can be a little smaller, taper toward its base, final task would be to make the bow bend slightly on full cock": pc-character bends the limbs slightly at full draw (the vertex bend keyed to the draw it held back); the quiver stays as shipped (Jeff, after seeing the tapered one: "actually quiver original is fine, revert to that"). **Visible work (Jeff, 2026-10-21 evening, his words): "town people need to be doing activities to better the town, chopping wood, going hunting, building a half constructed building, sleeping at night"**: the sim already has the routines, stints, work sites and the build order; what Jeff sees are bodies standing. Owner: npc leads the design (to the coordinator first) with economy (what each stint is: the woodcutter at the woodlot, the hunter walking out and back with game, the carpenter at the build, the farmer in the field), pc-character (work animations: chop, saw, hammer, carry, dig, sleep; tools in hand), buildings and builder (a building under construction drawn in stages as the build order progresses: a pad, a frame, walls, a roof) and art-kit (the stage pieces, scaffold, a woodpile, the carried log); at night residents go home and lie down (bodies inside, lamps out). Design approved 2026-10-21 (docs/visible_work.md, untracked): Resident gains a replicated "activity" (chop, carry_log, saw, hammer, sleep; phase 2 stalk, carry_game, hoe, herd, shear) set from the sim's state while npc moves the body to the work (the woodlot's spot during 521's trip, the build under construction, the bed at night); pc-character plays a clip per word with the tool in hand; buildings draws a build in stages by economy's build_progress in the town message (pad, frame, walls in quarters, roof); the lamp out per house by an awake map; art-kit's tools and pieces; builder's hint. Rulings: a building's walls block only from the walls stage; a planned building shows as a staked plot (Jeff's to overturn, in his folder). Phase 2 after Jeff sees phase 1. **The wilds and the town (Jeff, 2026-10-21 evening, his words): "wilds needs better land marks, in the wilds its tough to know where you are, the town doesn't need to be stuck so deep into the corner, where are the farms? - maybe the wilds stays wild and the town gets its own map thats more civilized"**: two things: landmarks in the wilds (maps with terrain: things seen from afar and told apart, a ridge line, a lone great tree, a cairn, a standing stone, the stream's course and a road, the escarpment cue that worked on the hills), and the town's own level (maps leads with lore, economy and builder: Held on a civilised map of its own, fields and farms seen as fields, pasture, roads, the well, the temple, the healer's hut, the mound's link and the wilds' link, the farms drawn where the sim has them); designs to the coordinator, the town level built only on Jeff's yes to the design (a new real level is content). Jeff (2026-10-22, his words): "i eventually want held to be in its own map that leaves room to grow, forests to chop down, town and city growing and becoming something is one of the major gameplay components - that means wilds will be its own area. the barrow will be on the map with Held": a yes to Held's own level, larger than the sheet's 128x128, with room to grow and forest to clear as the town grows (the woodcutter's work clearing real trees, the town taking the ground), the town growing into a city as a major gameplay component, the wilds its own area, and the barrow mound on Held's map (its wilds placement of 579 stands until Held exists). The layout pick (A, B or C) is still his. Jeff's layout (2026-10-22, his folder, his words): "Held design, river to west, barrow north west between but not across river, to east a road to wilds with farms in between town and wilds -- forest surrounds everything like map that can be expanded for more town or farm land, town can have fisherman that go to river and back with fish. snagfin the goblin can be on the other side of the river in his ruined fort": so Held's own layout: the river down the west side, the barrow mound north-west of the town between it and the river on the near bank, the gate road east to the wilds with the farms between the town and the wilds, forest all round to the edges for the clearing loop, a fisherman trade (economy: fish as food, the walk to the river and back), and Snagfin across the river in a ruined fort (builder and art-kit the fort as a ruin set piece, maps its place and a crossing, lore his story, economy's fence counter following his home). Maps' sheet (C:/dev/review/held/held_jeff.png: the river at x 34–44 with a ford north and a bridge by the town, the mound at 54–65 by 50–61 in a clearing on the near bank, the core 80–120 by 76–116 round a square with the well, the gate road east to the wilds' link at the east edge, the fishing spot by the bridge, the fort at 14–30 by 86–102) approved by Jeff 2026-10-22: "held_jeff is approved thats like what i wanted"; built after the clearing mechanism, a full cut with the world's migration. Jeff's details (2026-10-22, his words): "Held sit in the Woodlands"; "old farmstead in the wilds - abandoned, tools and bandages found inside"; "Hob's hut stays in the wilds near the castle - acts as respawn point if quest satisfied"; "Snagfins ruined fort is a broken down castle that the ancients lived in, was conquered in the battle and the fallen warriors buried before the people moved on - nothing known of their history of then they left."; "Snagfins fort called Old Tower". So: the land is the Woodlands (lore, the big map, the hails); the wilds' farmstead stays as an abandoned ruin with a small stash of tools and bandages inside (items' design approved 2026-10-22: a Chest entity in the ruin marked as a cache with a loot table, E on it a new "search" command (a protocol revision), the server rolling the table once per player for life, recorded on the player record, seeded by player and cache; bandaging kit ×2, a knife, a pick at half chance, minted worn with the origin "found in the old farmstead"; a ground pile for what the pack can't take; no key hint on screen; companions don't search; any level may mark a cache); Hob's hut stays in the wilds by the castle and is a return point once his quest is done (companions' shape approved 2026-10-22, 584 amended: when a player not in his care greets him or asks for work or news, Hob offers "Bring me five herbs from round my hut and I'll brew for you" (5 a draft), his hails mention it till done; five herbs handed to him over any visits puts the player in his care for good (economy's care mark), the herbs into his stock; npc the offer and count, economy the care rule "errand" for his hut, "retainer_or_errand" for the towns); the Old Tower across the river is a broken-down castle of the ancients, conquered in the battle, its fallen warriors buried (the Barrow of the Fallen on the near bank is their grave; lore ties the two), the people gone and nothing known of them since (lore writes the Old Tower entry on these words only; builder and art-kit build it as a broken castle tower, not a palisade fort). Jeff (2026-10-22, to builder): "no dead by the tower, the dead are in the barrows, the tower is where they lived": the tie to the Barrow of the Fallen stands, no graves at the tower. Maps' growth rework approved 2026-10-22: levels/held 192×192 (half the wilds' upkeep, room for weeks of growth), a 40×40 core in the picked layout (store, temple, forge, 5×5-minimum houses, well, green), forest and rough ground to the edges, the gate road to the wilds, the mound moved over with its link; the growth loop: each load the woodcutter takes clears forest cells at the lot's edge nearest the town (trees to stumps to grass, the woodlot stepping outward; economy's wood to maps' cells), cleared cells kept in the level's overlay and carried in the terrain message, cleared ground near the streets becoming plots (builder) and further out fields and pasture (economy), the town growing hamlet, village, town, city by its buildings and people, each stage unlocking kinds (buildings) and widening the curved wall; a new level and a revision line; the three layouts redrawn at the new scale for Jeff's pick. Jeff (same day, his words): "the server has to stay persistent once you set a worker to harvest they keep working, but forests can regenerate too if not built over": the clearing runs on the server's world tick whether anyone is near (the world-moves-on rule), a worker set to harvest keeps harvesting until told otherwise, and cleared ground not built over regrows slowly (stumps to scrub to saplings to forest over game seasons; the woodlot's edge moving back in), so a town that stops cutting is taken back by the forest. The growth loop approved whole 2026-10-22 (maps, buildings, economy): Clearing.fell on each woodcut felling the edge trees nearest the town (wood = trees × 2), the woodlot stepping out, cleared cells in the overlay and snapshot and the terrain message, regrowth a season a stage from the forest's edge in; Towns.stage rising hamlet, village (8 residents, a temple and an inn, the priest), town (16, the lawspeaker, walls, 150 gold, 100 wood), city (30, a second guard, a market, 400 gold), in the town message; Level.STAGES naming each kind's stage (hamlet huts, sheds, the healer's hut, a small inn and temple; village houses, forge, butchery, barn, stable, mill; town two-storey, longhouse, watchtower, the large temple, walls; city large sizes, stone, a market kind); plots in classes (S 6×6, M 8×12, L 13×16, long 16×6, plus a one-cell ring) opening when cleared and level, a home growing in place by its class keeping its id; order: the clearing, economy's fell calls and stage, buildings' stages and market, plot_for; Held's level itself on Jeff's pick. Jeff on the player's part (same day, his words): "no just that the player might have some involvement in how the town is working... maybe they can influence jobs, and can chip in themselves if the town is lacking. ie bringing in meat, bringing in wood": no direct orders to workers; the player can influence jobs (a word with the official or the worker, a say in what the town takes on) and can chip in: bringing meat, wood, herbs or hides to the store when the town is short, paid as a delivery; economy designs it after Held's first steps (what the town says it lacks, how the player hears it, what a delivery pays, how a job is influenced). Jeff (same day, his words): "a town status sheet will come up stating any pertinent stats of the town, the population, the faction, the needs, hunger etc.": a town sheet on screen (ui, with economy's figures in the town message: population, the faction, the stage, the needs and shortages, hunger and the dole, the treasury, the officials, what is being built), the place the town says what it lacks; ui designs it with economy, to the coordinator first. Maps' Held design (2026-10-21 evening, for Jeff's yes): today the town is the place "wilds/store" in the wilds' south-east corner, its field abstract with no cells, the fenced fields on the layout the old farmstead; the new level levels/held, about 128×128, outdoors, the core (store, depot and shop, temple, forge, kitchen, well, houses) moved as it is, plots on a street plan (builder), fields, a pasture and a woodlot as economy's site kinds so the farms get drawn cells, a stream and roads as curves (the roads piece lands first as the street layer), lore naming the land; a gate road linking to the wilds' south-east where the town is now, the mound staying in the wilds as a landmark 10–20 cells from the link, the start moving to Held; the wilds' corner given back to the wild with the landmark pass, the farmstead ruins or a hamlet (Jeff's pick); the box's saved world migrated on the server at load ("wilds/store" → "held/store", strays and a saved player placed in Held), a protocol bump, a full cut. Sheet to come in C:/dev/review/held/. Jeff on the sheet (2026-10-21 evening): "box's held is really bad design wise, its not even close to what the town should be, why are we stuck on that layout?": nothing binds the new map to the old layout but the sim's records (holdings, residents, purses, which carry over by id, not by cell); maps and builder draw three layouts from scratch for his pick or his sketch, with nothing of the old town's shape kept. Lore's lines: keep the region id town_lands, the land "Held's lands" until Jeff names it (his question: a name of its own, or the region's), the old farmstead best as ruins the Blood Rot burned (where the smith was taken from), Jeff's pick between ruins, a hamlet or gone. Economy's: the farm and woodlot inside Held near the walls, the hunting ground out in the wilds, fields drawn as plots per level with the crop word, a sheep pen by the pasture, the hunter hunting the ground's area, economy owning the world migration's place ids; Hob's hut outside the old town stays in the wilds unless Jeff wants it in Held. Builder's (accepted): a walled core about 56×56 with fields and sites outside, the wall a closed curve from the first pass (coordinator), two or three gates where the roads cross the ring (builder makes town_wall default to road crossings now), streets as road curves with a market square of about 16×16 holding the store, well and stalls, the temple on its north side, forge and depot by the main gate, houses facing lanes, 6–10 plots as a "plots" list with a door-on-a-street warning, new buildings into plots then outside the wall along roads. Landmarks design approved 2026-10-21 (maps, under Jeff's ask and the veto rule): the wilds are 256×256, flat, no streams, three small ponds, the only marks the town, the hut and the mound; the plan: a gentle roll with one long ridge across the north-east third carrying the escarpment cue (a 3–4 m face looking south-west with a way up) and a broad valley north-west to south-east; a river down the valley with a ford and a bridge, the ponds traced into curves, one fed as a small lake; a crossroads near the middle with roads to the castle, the hills, the forest and the south-east, a waystone at it; marks in clear ground: a lone great tree on a knoll, a cairn on the ridge, a ring of standing stones in the south-west, the farmstead as Jeff picks, the mound kept; art-kit's pieces, terrain's far view; the town rectangle untouched until Held is decided; each layer its own landing, a full cut. **Wolves (Jeff, 2026-10-21 evening, his words): "wolf packs of 4 near the town are too much, no packs in the wilds level. theres no way to survive that"**: the wolf herd (levels/economy.json herds, size 3–5 at density 0.25) leaves the wilds level entirely; packs elsewhere smaller; economy leads with maps (which herds a level carries) and enemy-ai (a pack's aggro); the box's existing packs culled when the level no longer lists them. Jeff approved the whole wolves draft 2026-10-22 ("approved": no wilds packs, 2–3 elsewhere at lower density never within 30 cells of a town building or link pad, a day hunt only at 4 together, leash 15, night sight 9; no longer drafts). Economy's plan approved: the wilds' level sets the wolf herd's multiplier to 0 (367 amended's per-level rule); elsewhere packs of 2–3 at density 0.15, kept 30 cells off every place and link pad so a pack is first seen about 26 cells out; a barred herd's groups, bodies and far-tier records culled on load with a count line, a wolf fleeing into a barred area gone; server only. Enemy-ai's side approved (drafts): a pack hunts a player by day only at 4 together (was 3), so the new packs of 2–3 leave players be unless struck; the chase leash 30 → 15 cells so a runner gets away; night sight 9 cells not 10. **Jeff's older answers (2026-10-22, his folder, his words): "1. farm animals do feed the town, but that employes the herder, the butcher, the cook - its a supply chain. 2. we dont need an hp number on the HUD. 3. bard could wear a hat yes. 4. no haggling right now. 5. main menu doesn't stays open when you select options so that's blocking the options screen. 6. rich tax can be 50%. 7. building sizes all need space inside, minimum 5x5 - no more 3x3 shacks".** Routed: (1) economy designs the meat chain (the flock's animals to the butcher, a new trade, to the cook; the shepherd as the herder); (2) ui removes the HP number from the HUD; (3) npc and art-kit give the bard a hat; (4) haggling closed; (5) ui fixes the main menu staying open over Options; (6) economy sets the rich's tax to 0.5; (7) buildings leads with builder, maps and economy: every building kind at least 5x5 with space inside, the 3x3 kinds regrown and the towns' plots and footprints to match. **Going down (Jeff, 2026-10-22, his folder, his words): "when a player is down, the game goes gray like now, and the heart beat becomes the dominant sound to the player; player will have two options on screen: have it say in big letters 'Mortally Wounded' two buttons, Slow Heart (Hold), Bleed Out (Respawn) - if you bleed out you return to the temple, the game says 'Aldous brought you back from near death', if you want to wait for your co-op or companion to revive you there, you can slow down your heart beat to keep yourself from bleeding out, until its safe, the companion then has to cast resurrect on you when conditions are met. sound effect wise when you go down you hear a heart beating, if you press Slow Heart, it drops if you keep pressing it it gets slower and slower from 120 to 80 to 40 bpm. If everyone dies you just automatically respawn a 60-90 seconds later but in the temple."** This replaces the death countdown's shape (396 amended): down is a state the player holds or leaves: the screen grey, the heartbeat the dominant sound, "Mortally Wounded" in big letters with two buttons, Slow Heart held (the heartbeat stepping 120, 80, 40 bpm, the bleed-out held off while held) and Bleed Out (the return to the temple at once, "Aldous brought you back from near death"); a blessed player held down is resurrected by a companion or a co-op player with a charge (573); everyone down means an automatic return at the temple after 60–90 s. Owner: companions leads the design (to the coordinator first) with ui (the grey, the words, the two buttons, the hold), sound (the heartbeat and its steps, dominant over the mix), economy (the temple return and the line; the all-down timer) and enemy-ai (what monsters do to a held-down player). Companions' design approved 2026-10-22 (C:/dev/review/companions/mortally_wounded_design.md): every player at 0 hp is Mortally Wounded; blessed: a 30 s window of blood at 120 bpm with Slow Heart (held: the beat steps to 80 and 40 every 2 s, the blood draining ×0.5 then ×0.2, about 150 s at most) and Bleed Out; unblessed: a 10 s last-moments window, Slow Heart greyed, Bleed Out only; Bleed Out or the blood out returns at once to the nearest manned temple or hut with the keeper's line; everyone down: "All have fallen" then 75 s and all return; the revive (her channel or a co-op player's held E with a charge) only when calm; monsters leave the downed alone; Player.heart replicated, slow_heart and bleed_out commands, the return message's near_death and all-fallen countdown; order: economy's return, companions' state, enemy-ai's confirmation, ui's screen, sound's heartbeat. Jeff (same day, his words): "yeah i kind of like the nearby healer hut idea.. but each healer would need to have been pre-paid or you do a small quest for them before you unlock that spawn point": a healer's hut is a return place for a player only once unlocked, by paying the healer ahead (a retainer) or by doing a small errand for him; the temple stays the default; economy designs the unlock (a per-player record of unlocked return places, the retainer's price, what the errand is drawn from npc's errands), npc the healer's words and the errand, ui the read; design to the coordinator first. Economy's design approved 2026-10-22: PlayerRecord.care lists the healers' huts a player is in the care of (kept through leaving and death); a hut counts as a return place only when in the player's care, temples always, the nearest manned wins, else the temple; Economy.retain takes the retainer (draft 10) into the healer's own purse and adds his hut; npc's errands done for him grant care without gold; the barter message carries a care row for a healer; a companion follows her player's care. **Labels and hints (Jeff, 2026-10-22, his words): "for npc's another change have their name on one line overhead, lets drop the profession -- don't show the mouse of tip for LMB to interact, try to remove any reference tooltips, it's going to be intuitive"**: the overhead label is the name alone on one line (the "Name [profession]" form of 2026-10-17 is dropped); the "LMB interact" cursor hint goes, and with it the other control-reference hints over things (the key hints that say which button does what); item names and histories on hover in the pack stay unless Jeff says otherwise. Owner: ui (the labels, hints and tips), npc told for the label. **Combat feel and archery (Jeff, 2026-10-22, his words): "ok today is going to be about combat feel and archery refinement. i want to try a few variations , one is a graphical fix. the arrow doesn't load into the bow with drawing, the actual bow and string look perfect -- for aiming bow and drawning back movement mouse disappears and the game draws a semi circle for distance, arrow can travel different ranges and closer range does more damage on full cock, but arrow physics on how it hits the ground or object needs to be cleaned up. aiming is right now locked by tile which is hard for the player. so use a circle which the aim can move along at many degrees, this adds more freedom to aim than the \"tile\" - clarify anything needed to build".** Four pieces: (1) the nocked arrow shown on the string through the draw (pc-character); (2) aiming while drawing: the cursor hidden, an arc drawn for the shot's range, the aim point moving along a circle at any angle rather than snapping to a tile (ui draws it, the sim takes an angle and a range: the shoot command changes, a protocol bump); (3) range by the draw and damage higher at close range on a full draw (the sim's shot: items or enemy-ai); (4) the arrow's landing cleaned up: sticking in ground and objects, not passing through, lying where it falls. Owner: pc-character leads the design (to the coordinator first) with ui, items and enemy-ai; clarifications asked of Jeff the same day. Jeff's clarifications (his words): "range grows the longer held back, but there gradients for distance, you can aim shorter for higher damage, not depending on bow cock - theres no throttle there - the range has increments, the arc goes along the semicircle, the arrow flies directly from tip to mouse point and hits whatever is in between, after the full range it sags down and lands in ground, not hitting ground at its target like it does now". So: the hold steps the range up through increments; the draw has no partial power; the damage is by the chosen distance, shorter is harder; the arc is drawn along the semicircle in front at the chosen range; the arrow flies straight from the bow's tip toward the mouse point, hitting the first thing between, and past its range it sags and lands in the ground, never striking the ground at the aimed point. Arrows (Jeff): "arrows dont need to be retrieved, they aren't used up.. just have them decay over 10 seconds like they do now". Design approved 2026-10-22 (pc-character's, with items, enemy-ai, ui): the ranged verb gains range steps reached by ticks of draw (draft [3, 5, 7, 9] cells, four steps over about 2 s) with damage per step, shortest hardest; try_loose takes an angle, a supercover ray from the shooter's centre to the step's range hitting the first body or blocker, friends spared; the views get the stop point and what stopped it (body, wood, stone, ground), a protocol bump; monster archers use the same call; ui hides the cursor while drawing, draws the arc at the step's radius jumping out per step, the marker on the arc at the mouse's angle, a damage read beside it; the client shows the nocked arrow from the draw's first frame (fixed), turns the archer to the aim continuously, flies the real arrow from the bow straight at bow height and, by what it struck, vanishes in a body, sticks in wood, glances off stone, or sags past its range into the ground; stuck arrows fade over 10 s. Defaults until Jeff answers: the arc follows the mouse, four steps over 2 s, a slow walk while drawn. **Sound (Jeff, 2026-10-22, v0.1.163, his words): "with the sound there an annoying noise that sounds like bugs or a hard drive noise...it goes away with ambience slider to 0"** (sound: an ambience bed or loop with a clicky grain or a bad seam; find it by listening to each take alone and fix). Found: the night bed's crickets take, chirping in the snow since nothing told the beds the season (578: the insect beds fade out as the year cools, gone where snow falls; three ticking water takes dropped). Jeff then: "the cricket noise is not good, it is a constant rattle, it's gonna drive me insane": the take itself goes, replaced by a sparse one with gaps and a lower register, or by none; a cut once it lands. **Jeff's play on the box, night of 2026-10-22 (v0.1.162):** "i cant get into the castle zone from wilds": the link cell (128,20) is fine in the level and on a local world; his walks stopped one cell short, so a body stood on it in the live world; release moved him by console; fix (maps, next cut): nothing rests on or aims for a link cell, and a step toward an occupied link cell takes the link. Jeff (2026-10-22, his words): "if maps is not busy queue up zone transitions in cooperation with dungeons and art-kit, there needs to be strong visual cues of where the zone transition is, descending/ascending steps, arches, etc.": every link between areas gets a built cue at its cell: a gate arch or a waystone on an overland link, a stair down or up where a level sits below or above, a door or a cut in a wall, the cue placed by the level data from the link's kind, art-kit's pieces, dungeon's stairs for its layers; maps leads the design (the link kinds and their cues, placement by the loader), to the coordinator first, after 579 and the castle's water. (Built water, 2026-10-22, maps and terrain: maps first read the castle's 41 water cells as stone basins inside walls, then corrected itself: they are cell-painted ponds on grass and are traced into curves under 568 amended with the sample's pool and stream; what stays is the rule for wells and any true basin, square with a crisp stone edge and still water, that water no "waters" curve lays is built (a basin, a well's cell, a cistern), computed on the client from the water cells minus the curves' cells, no level data and no protocol line, with maps' level_test check that every curveless water body in a shipped level stands beside floor, wall or building cells, so a stray cell-painted pond fails the suite instead of drawing square; the curve rule is for natural water: rivers, streams, shorelines, falls.) Maps' design approved 2026-10-22: the cue picked by Level from the link's kind (overland: a gate arch about 4 m tall with a light at dusk, or paired waystones where an arch won't fit, the road through; down: a stair-down cut with a dark mouth; up: a stair or climb; sealed or hidden: the slab, the stair once found), a "cue" override on a link entry, the piece standing on the link's cell facing the way out, drawing only (the cell stays floor, the posts scenery), the loader writing cue and facing into the links, Client3D placing art-kit's piece, the big map marking each link with its icon and where it goes; a revision line, a full cut; the castle link at (128,20) first. "i died in the castle and not respawning": the return worked but took 70 s with no word on screen (ui: a HUD line for the bring-back with its wait; the return's owner says whether 70 s is by design); and the castle, waking after 19 hours asleep, respawned 10 monsters in two minutes round him (enemy-ai: timers don't accrue while a zone sleeps, nothing respawns in sight of a newly arrived player). Then two deaths in the barrow each segfaulted the server within seconds (systemd restarted it; companions leads the repro with dungeon, economy and enemy-ai, perf reads the coredump's stack on the box; the cut held up to an hour for the fix). "killed a brute and sneak and the corpses had no palette" (pc-character: the corpse path loses the hand-built bodies' paint). His castle shot: Pip's party lines leaked a name list ("Wren, Aldous, Snagfin. The boar is moving closer.") and called the fight lost mid-fight (companions reads the mind log); the party log carried the town's dole line in the castle and the screenshot's full path (ui quiets both); health bars over Talos and Pip against the no-bars rule (Jeff's call). **The healer's stamina potion (Jeff, 2026-10-21 evening, his words): "hob can have a stamina potion that improves run speed and trades for herbs - other npcs don't find herbs useful"** (Hob is the medicine man in his world; the rule is the trade's, not the name's): the medicine man makes and sells a stamina potion that raises run speed for a while, buys herbs, and is the only resident who does; herbs have no price at any other counter ("won't buy"). Owner: economy leads (the healer's stock and the herb rule, under the barter window's phase 2), with items (the potion item, its effect and duration, the drinking) and npc (the healer's make from herbs beside the remedy); design to the coordinator first. Design approved 2026-10-21 (one number, one bump): items' stamina_potion (a small stoppered flask, a "speed" buff +0.25 for 30 minutes (Jeff: "try 30 minutes not two"), drunk from the pack, a second resets the timer, the HUD "Quick 25%", 3 herbs and 600 steps so about 4 gold); made on the medicine man's own tick beside the remedy, a shelf of 3, the remedy first when someone is sick, his own gathering unchanged so players' herbs are the real supply; the barter commands take a resident in place of a shop, his shelf as goods, his recipes' materials bought from his own purse up to his shelf's need, herbs elsewhere "the medicine man buys herbs"; found and fixed on the way: the remedy's recipe named a trade that doesn't exist ("healer" for "medicine_man"), so no remedy was ever made; numbers drafts for Jeff. Economy's 30-day soak (2026-10-22): identical to the baseline without barter; found Hob's gathered herbs went to the town's store, not his hut (fixed, 502 amended) and that herbs grow only in rings round town stores, 56 cells from his hut, so he gathered nothing; coordinator's ruling: a herb ring round each healer's hut as round a store; players stay the real supply. **Interaction, trade and chat rework (Jeff, 2026-10-21 evening, his words): "npc interaction needs to work with UI on a trade/barter and chat rework - i don't like how thing currently work. lmb opens up chat with the npc name, UI says LMB to chat; clicking should be 'LMB to interact' - and the npc stops, faces, you, and addresses you. If you click their chat bubble that could open up the 'text bar below' that confirms you didnt just accidentally click them you want to talk to them - i want a couple options for bartering, when clicking bartering the window should show the npcs inventory of goods, next your pack, hovering over an item would show what the npc will pay in gold, or what theyll take in gold. direct trading should be possible too. click to select what you want to trade, it would show a sum in the bottom, ie npc has a sword for 10, you have a hide for 20, highlight both, the button changes to +sword -hide +10g = Confirm".** Owner: ui leads the design (to the coordinator first), with npc (the stop, face and address through the spoken path; the bubble), economy (prices both ways, direct trades, the trade's wire) and pc-character (the body turning to face). This widens 536's no-panel rule: the talk stays spoken, but a click on the bubble opens the text bar, and barter is a window. Design approved 2026-10-21 evening (ui's, with npc's, economy's and pc-character's lines): the hint "LMB interact"; a click in reach sends npc's "greet": the resident stops about 8 s, turns to the player (pc-character's standing turn and look-at on a replicated Resident.addressing) and speaks a short table word at once by trade and standing, then their real hail of the day if the mind is free (554's limits; the click brings the hail forward), refusals at most "Not now."; the addressed resident's bubble is clickable and opens the text bar with Talk (536's say line) and Barter; the barter window (BarterPanel, replacing ShopPanel's buy row and selling into the counter; 438's CounterPanel stays): their goods over the pack, hover "takes 10 gold" or "pays 20 gold" or "won't buy", click selects on either side, the foot's signed sum and one button "+sword -hide +10g = Confirm"; economy prices it on the server all or nothing (Economy.barter_quote, up_to caps, a stale sum refused "the prices have moved"), phase 1 merchants only, phase 2 makers' benches approved to follow; coordinator's rulings, Jeff's to move: grave goods and stolen things fenced at half, never refused; haggling waits on Jeff. Jeff overturned the fence ruling (2026-10-22, his folder, his words): "Snagfin could be the only fence, he will buy stolen and grave goods, no one else wants them, he has a little larger place on the outskirts of town, he might only do that at night, he might refuse to buy stolen goods unless its night time, he sells overpriced goods during the day and wont buy stolen goods unless its night time and at a fenced goods discount": Snagfin alone fences, at night only and at a fenced discount; by day he sells overpriced goods and refuses stolen and grave goods; every other counter says "won't buy"; his place a little larger on the outskirts (maps/builder, and in Held's design). Owner: economy (the rule and prices), npc (his words by day and night), ui (the hover words), maps and builder (his place). Town npcs (Jeff, 2026-10-20, from a screenshot: "town npc verbalize their thoughts"): what leaked was the model's stage direction around the speech; then "might be interesting if theres some sort of narration quality, such as 'Jeff, I've been meaning...' his voice steady despite the rain": the spoken words are the bubble, the narration turned into one third-person clause after the speech in the talk log (how it was said, never an action that didn't happen, never a thought), enforced by code (npc, ui for the log's style). Archery: pc-character fixes the bow and bow arm clipping the torso first (the hand socket per body width, an aim layer lifting the arm out, a clearance check across the five builds), then the held pose, the turn to the target, the draw, the release and recoil as one sequence, a strip for Jeff before landing.

The minimap turns with the camera (Jeff, 2026-10-19: "the minimap should probably rotate with compass and map, because it gets confusing, and the player arrow doesn't seem consistent with what direction one is facing"): up on the minimap is up on the screen, as the compass turns, and the player arrow points the player's own facing (ui, with maps).

Talk has no panel (Jeff, 2026-10-19, after play tests: "the npc interaction ui, the questions at the bottom don't seem natural when every npc is the same... i'd rather not have the ui at all"): the quick-ask row goes first; then conversation with no panel, the resident's words as speech and the party log, an offer spoken as a line taken by a typed word or a single shown key, the shop and counter kept as the store's own panels, what the player owes in the pack or the log. Design approved 2026-10-19 (ui and npc): the panel goes; E or a click addresses the say line to a resident; a line asking for work, wares or news gets the matching offer's words spoken as the body's speech and the log, one standing offer per player and resident, taken by "yes", "I'll take it", "done" or the name (last spoken wins), amounts spoken for the building fund and loans ("the rest", "give 25", "lend 5"); the HUD says what "yes" takes with a rebindable key; open commissions as "Promised: ..." in the pack's foot; the shop and counter stay the store's own panels; nothing volunteered on a resident's own initiative.

## The barrow: the starter dungeon (Jeff, 2026-10-19, `docs/lore/crypt.md`)

Jeff's lore page, draft, his lines marked [Jeff]: a barrow a little outside Held, ancient, older than the town, whose founders plundered its grave goods for the gold that founded the town and shoved the old dead deeper; the old gods sent the draugr, and the dead only walk home to their graves. It is a delve-again place like the labyrinths, filled with tombs, reanimated skeletons raised by the draugr's curse, grave robbers after the altar's gold, an altar room where the temple's offerings are laid and a hidden passage to the draugr's lair, a small boss at the end. Until blessed, being brought back at the temple costs a gold offering; Aldous keeps the temple, takes alms and sends them down (his secret; the town shuns the crypt out of superstition), his apprentice Pip carries the gold in secret and the last runner never came back. Give the draugr gold and he offers a deal: beat him and be blessed. Blessing once gives the draugr's two-part device (the player's item, the companion's return token, 5, 10 or 15 uses by the difficulty he was beaten at, one count per blessed player spent on any blessed body including oneself): a blessed player is brought back in the field, a fallen companion by the player standing still and unhit for a few seconds; the unblessed start again in town. A shortcut from the temple to the draugr's room, sealed, opens per player only for the blessed, and a blessed player may bring a companion through it, but every player must run the barrow and beat the draugr once. The barrow is the starter dungeon: the blessing is what you need before venturing far from town.

**Jeff's play test on v0.1.163 (2026-10-22, his words): "winding river is good, new barrows graphic didn't make the cut. crypt is still too cramped.. its okay if its not realistic, make the first room a large room with a burial table, slots in the walls with ancient skeletons, these don't need to be animated. there would be a hidden door that leads to deeper much larger structures... at least 2-3 layers down.. its ancient and mysterious not built by the town, the grave robbers are on the first layer, the undead on the second, and finally the draugr has his own boss room that is large with room to maneuver and use strategic positioning".** The mound: his hand-built piece was still waiting on his size pick; the coordinator lands it at 12x12 at maps' west spot with art-kit's trim and thickened spears unless he says otherwise. Jeff confirmed (2026-10-22, his folder, his words): "12×12 at the west spot. Art-kit trims in its copy: kerb in to ~0.85 so it sits at the dome's foot, bring the 4 outer spears inside ~6.2 m. Thicken the spears 2.5× with bigger heads; keep the count and I'll judge from new trial shots whether to thin them out. 4,600 tri budget approved. Art-kit's copy is now the master; export and land it under my name." Then (his words): "the trial images are good to go live, i want the barrow outside town, not at the map edge, it double as a travel landmark": maps places the mound in open ground outside the town, off the map's edges, on the south-east road between the town's corner and the waystone, in sight from the road, the link and arrival moving with it. The Crypt reworked (dungeon leads, design to the coordinator first, with art-kit, enemy-ai, lore, maps): the first room a large hall with a burial table and wall slots holding ancient skeletons as props; a hidden door to deeper, much larger structures, two or three layers down, ancient and not the town's; grave robbers on the first layer, the undead on the second, the draugr in a large boss room of his own with room to manoeuvre and position; realism gives way to play. Dungeon's design approved 2026-10-22 (C:/dev/review/dungeon/barrow_layers_design.md): three levels in one place: barrow (layer 1, 80x60, regenerating: the first hall 26x16 under the mound with the burial table and 14 skeleton niches, lit and empty of foes, then the robbers' halls), barrow_deep (layer 2, 100x80, regenerating: ways 5 wide, rooms to 16x12, the tombs, the altar room 18x14 with its guards, Pip's niche, a stair down) and barrow_lair (layer 3, 44x34, hand-drawn, fixed: an antechamber where the temple's shortcut now ends, the hall 32x22 with six pillars, a dais and side bays); the hidden door one of the niches, picked each generation, a crack above as the tell, its slab giving when stepped into, open for all until the next generation (links gain hidden, the map message carries the zone's found tiles). Steps: the levels and generator knobs, the hidden door, the hall drawn, enemy-ai's foes by layer; sheets and shots before each. Jeff (2026-10-22, his folder, on the barrow's size): "halls need to be at least 2 tiles wider than the walls, this is because otherwise all you see is wall. the rooms are designed for space even though its less realistic the gameplay is the top priority": a rule for every layer: a way's clear width is at least the wall's drawn thickness plus two cells, so the floor shows past the walls from the game camera; the foes' numbers stand as drafts. Reworked (580, dungeon, step 1 built 2026-10-22; plan sheets in C:/dev/review/barrow_layers/): barrow (80x60: the first hall with the burial table and 14 niches holding the town's newer dead, the stair down in one niche, the altar room with its guards and Pip's niche, the town's tombs, the robbers' camp, 4 robbers), barrow_deep "The Ossuary" (100x80, the ancient dead's tombs by depth, 6 skeletons), barrow_lair "Hall of Fallen Souls" (100x80, dark caverns without doors or torches, the oldest dead, the hall 32x22 with pillars, the round table, the dais and throne, the temple's shortcut ending behind it as the hidden exit); a wall draws 0.5 m thick and 3 m tall and hides about 2.5 cells behind it, so every way is five cells clear and rooms 8x7 to 16x12; a near-wall lowering drafted by terrain if it still reads as wall. Jeff answered lore's four questions (2026-10-22, his folder, his words): "1. no that was just a design text, the lore remains that the top floor is the newer dead, there could be several rooms off the main altar room too, but only the wealthy get the barrow, the poor are in pauper's graves outside. 2. the ancient dead were moved to the second layer could be called The Ossuary, the grave robbers would have gold on them, either from stealing or from other jobs. third layer, where the oldest dead were moved as far as the town people dared go, its damp and dark, human eyesight barely lights it up, its too moist to light lamps.. the dead are angry about being disturbed and they are animated by the magic of the Draugr. 3. the Draugr's hall can be 'Hall of Fallen Souls' it could be set up like castle hall, with a round table with chairs around it, pillars, broken tables at the sides, and the hidden exit behind the throne -- the hall is filled with ghostly warriors that were promised eternal feast and battle. 4. lets change the plunder from the towns people to graver robbers.. its just that the dead donk know the difference". So the layers are: the top floor the town's newer dead (the wealthy only; the poor in pauper's graves outside the mound), several rooms off the altar room; the second layer the Ossuary, the ancient dead moved there, the grave robbers carrying gold; the third the oldest dead moved as far as the town dared, damp and dark, eyesight barely lighting it and lamps unlightable, the dead angry and animated by the draugr's magic; the draugr's Hall of Fallen Souls like a castle hall with a round table and chairs, pillars, broken tables at the sides, the hidden exit behind the throne, filled with ghostly warriors promised eternal feast and battle; the plunder the grave robbers', not the town's. Dungeon reworks the layers' content on this, lore its entries, enemy-ai the ghostly warriors and the third layer's dead, art-kit the hall's furniture, terrain and environment the third layer's darkness (no lamps), maps the pauper's graves outside. Jeff on the old gods (2026-10-22, his folder, his words): "the old gods are what grants the draugr his power, his ability to grant you the old gods blessings... people forgot how to worship the old gods, they are gods of war, and they will use their power to send the dead back into battle, thats why they give you the power of resurrection, they are angered by the cowardly robbers who just pilfer.. they are appeased by gold offers because it returns whats stolen. the town cleric Aldous has knowledge of this and keeps the town safe... his temple goers don't know they are donating to the old gods." Lore records it as agreed; the draugr's offer words and Aldous's secret follow it (enemy-ai, npc). **Res tokens (Jeff, 2026-10-22, pasted spec, his words, for the next cut): "The Crypt. It's the first dungeon, entered through the barrow mound. That's where companions get blessed. The blessing. It's permanent and unlocks resurrecting companions outside town. Before the blessing, resurrection only happens in town. Res tokens. Each resurrection in the field costs a token. You earn tokens by beating the barrows, and harder settings pay more: 5, 10, or 15. Scaling difficulty. The Crypt gets harder the more tokens you hold. At 50 tokens it's much tougher. Beat it and you're at 65, and it gets tougher again. Eventually the player decides 'that's enough for now,' so the stopping point is the player's choice rather than a hard cap. Capacity. The maximum number of tokens you can hold grows over the game, like heart containers in Zelda. That gives you permanent progression that's resilience rather than power. Still open: What a failed high-tier run costs. Do you lose tokens and slide back, or can you retry with no penalty? In co-op, whose stockpile sets the difficulty: yours, Dire's, or the combined total? Where capacity upgrades come from: bosses, rescued characters, or town upgrades."** This replaces the earlier drafts on the blessing (renewal, for life, the pact's difficulty by gold given): the blessing is permanent and per player; a field resurrection (544's channel) costs one token; tokens are a per-player count with a capacity; the barrow's difficulty (the foes of 545/547/561, the draugr's retinue) scales by tokens held in three settings paying 5/10/15; beating the barrow is the draugr laid to rest. Owner: dungeon leads the design (to the coordinator first) with companions (the blessing and the token spent on a field resurrection), enemy-ai (the three settings and the scaling by tokens held), economy (the token count, its capacity and upgrades: not gold, not items), ui (the count, the capacity and the barrow's setting read on screen, diegetic). Coordinator's defaults until Jeff answers the three open questions: a failed run costs nothing (retry free); in co-op the difficulty follows the highest holder in the party; capacity grows by beating a harder setting for the first time. Jeff's correction (2026-10-22, his words): "i think you misunderstood my point, draugr gives the blessings to any player or companion that takes the deal and wins. before blessing you respawn in the town at the temple. after you have the blessing you can be resurrected by a companion as long as you have a ressurection charge token counter, its not an item, its part of you, it doesn't trade. The interesting part was the the draugr gives more charges on higher difficulties, you can choose the difficulty by how much gold you donate. lets say 20, 50, 100 for 5, 10, and 15 res charges. the catch is the draugrs level is equal to base level + how many player ressurection points are in the zone when he starts the fight. if player zone in while the fight is active, he heals 50% of his health immediately. once you have a charge you can ressurect anyone that is dead, but it drains an active res token from you." So, against dungeon's first design: the blessing goes to whoever takes the draugr's deal and wins, player or companion; the setting is chosen by the gold donated to the draugr (20/50/100 for 5/10/15 charges), not at the door and not free; the only scaling is the draugr's own level, base plus the sum of resurrection charges held by the players in the zone when the fight starts (no tier table scaling the halls); a player zoning in mid-fight heals him 50% at once; a holder of a charge can resurrect anyone dead, draining one of their own charges. Capacity as in his first spec. Landed whole as 573 on 2026-10-22 (economy d0915aa6, dungeon 3d7d7875 with Player.charges and charge_room on the wire, revision 30, enemy-ai e6f07d99, ui 34b68024 "charges 23 of 50" in the pack, companions adfd70bc); the coordinator's ruling from Jeff's words: only the blessed get the field window; drafts in his folder (a failed pact costs the gold alone, capacity 25/50/75 by the hardest deal won, the halves no longer minted). Jeff's answer (2026-10-22, his words): "I don't want the draugr to explain everything in one sentence. Make it somewhat of a puzzle to figure out. The door closes when you enter. \"Why have you entered my Hall, \"name\"?\" after a pause, he says, \"Return the gold your people stole from this place, when you do that you may leave\". Depending on how much gold you turn in you get a different result, for < 20 gold, he attacks and if you kill him the door opens and he says \"...warrior, you have my respect.\" if you turn in the right amounts of gold you get resurrection tokens. He says \"...you have honored the pact, I shall give you the power to cheat death\" the first time you kill him you get a Draugr's Totem that holds the charges and the item explains \"carrying this totem allows you to recieve another chance to fight\"." So 573 amended, dungeon leading with enemy-ai, items, companions and lore: the door closes on entry, his two lines with a pause and nothing explained (the amounts are the puzzle), gold handed to him, under 20 a fight at his base with no pact and the respect line opening the door, at a deal the fight as before and the pact line on a win, the first win giving the Draugr's Totem that holds the charges (bound, never traded, dropped, sold or lost on death: his "part of you" stands, only the container changed), later wins adding to it, the item's note his words; his drafted speech with the amounts is out. Dungeon's design approved 2026-10-22: the hall's one door shuts behind the first player in while he sits (a server rule on the door edge, "The door is shut.", the shut look on the map message with 580 step 2's found state), opening when he's laid to rest by a pact, falls unpaid, or the fight is lost, never for a player who pays nothing and won't fight (gold, his death, or the temple return are their ways); his two lines through the offer contract, a pause of about 3 s, amounts never said, gold handed by saying an amount; under 20 or struck unpaid he keeps the gold and fights at his base, beaten he yields at 1 hp with the respect line and the door opens, no charges; 20/50/100 the deal as 573, the pact line on a win, the first win minting the totem and every win adding to it; the totem instance holds the charges, bound, never traded, dropped, sold or lost, its note "n of capacity", the field return and can_revive reading it, a blessed record from 573 given a totem on join; order 580 step 1, items, dungeon, enemy-ai and companions.
