## Companion rewards, guides, loyalty and leaving (Jeff, 2026-10-20, `docs/lore/companion_rewards.md`, status open)

Jeff's lore page, everything on it open for change, his lines marked [Jeff]: each area culminates in a companion-centred reward the way the barrow ends in the blessing; besides gold and loot, finishing an area affects loyalty and gives the companion who was there a permanent ability or trait (his example: the bow, learned at an elven outpost in the woods); a companion who has been to a place can guide you there, you following her on autopilot over the world's real ways, interrupted when danger shows, and next time she could refuse because the road isn't safe; areas affect loyalty; from his students: a companion should change her behaviour by what you do and could leave. Proposals on the page (not Jeff's yet): three payouts per area; abilities taught by a people or a place so a build comes from history (archer, healer, shieldbearer by where you took her), with a limit on how many so the choice of whom to bring matters; a teachers table (the draugr, elves, the dwarves' halls, the rot, the medicine man, the dark elves' tower); guides from what she knows, refusing by temper and what she heard, the world answering it; loyalty shown in behaviour; leaving only for what the player did, with warning signs and steps, home to town and findable, the legacy three never enemies.

Owner: `companions` leads, with `maps` (the guide's travel over WorldRoutes and its interruption), `lore` (teachers and places), `economy` (the area's gold and loot payout), `enemy-ai` (what an ability changes in a fight). Design to the coordinator first, the guide first since the routes exist and the barrow's blessing is the pattern's first instance; the ability catalog after Jeff settles the limit. Guide design approved 2026-10-20 (companions, three phases): her roads as what she saw with her player (areas and places, captivity counted), the walk on WorldRoutes with Heel's roles swapped and Main.travel taking the pair through, control back to the player on any input, on danger (a monster that would fight them, a raid group), on a hit, a fall, a shut way or arrival; a replicated led_by (protocol bump); refusal when the route's dangers (lived, seen, heard, her fears) outweigh her boldness by temper plus the bond and the party, fading with days and safe trips, offering to go as far as the leg before the danger; the world answering (a camp taken clears it); birthplaces when lore has places. Coordinator's rulings: real time at run pace, no time-skip in a shared world; her own player only; talking her round later. Phase 1 landed 2026-10-21 (57a46bfe, Guide, protocol 28; npc's road news to companions 8ed933ef; ui's reads, "551, ui's side", built: the HUD line while led, a mark on the big map beside every place she knows, a click to ask her). Phase 2 approved 2026-10-21 (Jeff: "Next time, she could refuse: it isn't safe, there are bandits on that road"): npc owns what she knows of a road (RoadKnowledge: a heard danger from a raid on her news, weighted 2 and fresh 4 days, lived ones 8, fading to nothing at twice that; a camp nobody holds drops its heard dangers on sight; a leg led through without a stop halves an area's dangers once a day; a fact clause for her voice, "there are bandits on the Fen road: the goblins struck the wool wagon four days back"); companions owns the refusal (checked at the ask and each new leg, boldness by temper plus the bond tier, the same goal refused again for the rest of the day, told to her player or in her town), the words and the stop. Landed 2026-10-21 (npc's RoadKnowledge 7155a1c3; companions' 630790fc; led_to on the wire ce3f31b4): she refuses a road whose dangers reach her nerve, names the worst, offers to go as far as the last place short of it, and stops partway if the road turns bad. Drafts for Jeff: boldness by temperament fearless 5, default 3, wary or careful 2; the bond wary -1, fond 0, close 1, devoted 2; +1 for each other player nearby. Phase 3 (birthplaces) waits on lore's places. Next (coordinator, 2026-10-21, design first): loyalty shown in behaviour and the warning signs before leaving, nothing random, every drop naming what the player did; leaving itself waits on Jeff's answers. Approved 2026-10-21 (companions, on 444's bond and Standing's ladder, every move a bump naming what the player did, drafts for Jeff): steps devoted 6, close 3, fond, cooling -1, wary -3, sullen -5, apart -7, leaving waits; devoted keeps a cell closer and stands over her downed player, wary and below hang back, join a fight late and retreat sooner; grave goods refused by the superstitious below close, carried at a cost at close, freely when devoted (items marks them in the history, dungeon sets it); close and devoted volunteer to guide, sullen refuses every guide ask; drops: left to fall alone -1, let bleed out unchanneled -2, grave goods -1, taken where she said wasn't safe -1 (with 444's struck and kept from); raises: the draugr laid to rest together +2, a camp cleared with her +1 (with 444's rescued, gift, fed, bandaged, days, stood by); warning signs by step, said in her voice, hanging back, refusing roads and loads; at apart her resting body sits apart in town and the gossip says she is unhappy (npc); a sullen companion still takes a gift and food, the amends path (coordinator). Landed 2026-10-21 (d2d3918e, with items' grave mark and npc's cdfbf35c; ui's read 8509865c: the step in the pack's title with the reason on hover, a HUD hint once per drop). The proof page C:/dev/review/companions/loyalty.md (devoted against wary through one barrow fight) asks Jeff: does one blow take devoted to close; how fast should gifts mend a sulk (eight gifts and food in the proof). Open for Jeff: can a companion learn everything or only a few; does each reward open the next area; does a leaver take what she earned; how far leaving goes. Jeff answered 1 and 2 (2026-10-22, in his decisions folder, his words): "1. companions do learn everything -- developing concept: ability is more like metroidvania, each helps you in higher difficulty areas, for example resurrection helps you in exploring other areas - and each area in turn add capability; an example would be visting the elven lands for an ability that counters 'rot' allowing you to explore the deeper areas of the blood rot caverns. 2. what makes companions distinct is their personality cards, trade abilities, what combination of fighting, magic, and stealth they tend to use.. since there is no class, they develop strength food eating and fighting, magic from using magic, wearing magical charms, and meditating, stealth users gain in dexterity by using bows, throwing, and games of wit - theres a balance in the form of a spider chart where you only have say 100% focus, and growing positively can pull from the others". So: abilities are capabilities an area grants that open harder areas (the blessing and resurrection the first; a rot counter from the elven lands opens the deeper Blood Rot caverns), learned by everyone who earns them; distinctness is the card, the trade and a three-way focus (fighting, magic, stealth) that grows by use and pulls from the others. Owner: companions leads the design (to the coordinator first) with enemy-ai (what each capability changes in play; the focus's effect on a fight), lore (the teachers and places), items and economy (charms, food, bows and games of wit as the things that train each focus), ui (the focus read as a diegetic chart on her card, never numbers on the HUD). Companions' design approved 2026-10-22 (C:/dev/review/companions/abilities_focus_design.md): a capability is a mark with an entry in levels/capabilities.json (words, area, teacher, holders, gates), the draugr's live, rot_ward, dwarf_craft, healer_lore and dark_gift drafts until their areas exist; Main.grant_capability generalises the barrow's win; gates party-level (a sealed way opens when the player or the companion with them holds the mark; hostile air, locked crafts and countered monster kinds built with their first area); the focus {fight, magic, stealth} summing to 100 on her card, floor 5, 3 a day, a gain drawn from the others, trained by what exists (food, blows, kills, bow shots) and later charms, meditating, throwing and games of wit, one multiplier per kind for enemy-ai's effects, ui's three-point chart on her card; nothing replicated per tick. Order: catalog and gates, focus, ui's chart, enemy-ai's effects, content on Jeff's ask. Defaults for Jeff: party-level gates, no player focus yet. Jeff answered the leaving questions (2026-10-22, his folder, his words): "1. companion keeps their blessings and abilities but goes back to their home town to live and work their vocation. 2. companions could, based on their personality, come back if you build favor back up with them, say your reputation improves, if they are justice driven, and you repair that they could be recruited again - in the mean time you may have to take a different companion or go solo until you do that. 3. companion would never become an enemy, but there might be whole towns that switch factions in the future -- if that happens the companion would decide based on their loyalty if they are coming with you - they could join your faction permanently". So leaving: at apart she goes home to her town and works her vocation as a resident, keeping her blessings and abilities; she can be recruited again when favour is rebuilt by what her personality cares about (reputation, repaired wrongs for the justice-driven); never an enemy; a town switching factions later makes her choose by loyalty, and she may join the player's faction for good. Owner: companions designs leaving (to the coordinator first) with npc (her resident life while away, her trade), economy (her vocation's work), lore (reputation as the town knows it); the faction switch is a later system. Companions' leaving design approved 2026-10-22 (C:/dev/review/companions/leaving_design.md): she leaves at apart at the next calm moment (the last straw while sullen or colder: let bleed out), says why, walks out of the zone and becomes a resident of her home town working her trade through the resting path (record "left"; her own things stay, the player's return); keeps her card, marks, blessing, focus and roads; favour rebuilds by time, the town's word of the player (doubled for the justice-driven), gifts on a visit, an errand for her kin, and for the justice-driven the wrong repaired by deed (a repairs table); she'll go again at wary, rejoining at cooling when asked in her town; never hostile; the recruit path for another companion is the next design after leaving lands; drafts in Jeff's folder. 

## How it plays (design rules — don't violate without asking Jeff)

- **No classes, no XP treadmill.** What you carry and how you play decide what you're good at.
- **Weapons are verbs**, not damage tiers:
  - Hammer: knockback (high push force).
  - Sword & shield: block arc in facing direction absorbs damage and pushes at stamina cost; heavy hits break guard.
  - Giant axe: slow, two-handed, 3-cell arc, knocks targets down ~1.5 s (prone: can't act or resist pushes).
  - Spear: hits two cells away; braced, stops a charge.
  - Dagger: fast, low damage, +100% from behind (uses facing).
  - Bow: range; longer draw = stronger shot.
- **You become what you do** — slowly. Habits shift the body (heavier/leaner/sharper mind); it's a trade, not a climb (heavy is slow, light is easy to throw). Two hands = choices (sword or the staff that feeds mind).
- **Gear is kept; buffs are earned.** Weapons are never consumable. Food, magic items and social time (cards with a local) give temporary buffs, found in town or on the road (larders, libraries, puzzles); companions remind you when they fade. Buffs never drop you below baseline.
- **Items have history.** Each item records carriers (with time held) and up to five notable events. Later: items take the name of a long-time holder ("Wren's dagger") and slowly absorb that holder's strength (capped, slow, drifts to a new owner). Record history from the start (the `items` worktree makes items instances), even before naming is built.
- **The town is the starting point** (Jeff, 2026-10-14): new players start in the town
  (Jeff is laying it out in builder mode); the start zone and cells are data in the levels,
  not a server flag.
- **Only heavy blows knock back** (Jeff, 2026-10-14): a blow slides its target only when its
  force clears a threshold (`World.knockback_min_force`); lighter hits do damage without a
  slide. Shoves, falls and door impacts are unchanged. The hammer's verb clears it.
- **Death and return** (Jeff, 2026-10-14): raiders and monsters can kill; players and
  companions are brought back through a medicine man or a temple in town. (The free
  respawn is a placeholder until they exist.)
- **A flowing town economy** (Jeff, 2026-10-15): the treasury starts at 0 and 150 gold is
  spread evenly across the residents' purses; townspeople trade items and gold among
  themselves for services and food, so gold circulates on the world tick rather than
  pooling; the town's gold is a bank, not a hoard: it collects taxes and fees as capital and
  lends the shortfall to a resident in need (a cap, small interest, repaid from income or
  worked off as town labour), pays official jobs only (guards, haulers) and town-ordered
  work; makers earn from what they sell. Gold is personal; rent (1 a day) and upkeep are
  the sinks; gold is never created, only moved.
- **The long game is the world**, not the character: town grows with rescued people, enemies hold and retake ground, companions remember.
- **Companion prompts say what's true and what to do, never what to say.**
- **Licensing:** no assets with non-commercial or regional restrictions; record every third-party asset/tool/model in `THIRD_PARTY.md` with a `source` per asset.

## Backlog (decided or agreed ideas, not yet scheduled)

Gathered from the whole project history. Items marked *idea* are brainstorms Jeff liked
but hasn't committed to; confirm before building them.

### Game loop, town and world
- **Town as the progression** (now the `economy` worktree). Everything found on a run (gold, ores, weapons) goes to the town stockpile. Rescued people join the town as shopkeepers, guards, the smith; the town has its own experience that pays for upgrades. Not every companion is a rescued prisoner — they're met in varied ways.
- **Companion growth.** Companions gain experience from a run but only keep it if they make it back to town alive (and grow when a run is won); fallen companions can be brought back in town. Players have no XP; their growth is the body shape above.
- **No runs** (Jeff, 2026-10-14: runs were a relic of an earlier dungeon-delve loop). The town grows from rescues and accomplishments, such as a story objective met (bringing the smith home); losing a fight never takes away what's already banked. Not punitive. Companions left in town work a trade as residents.
- **Enemies win by holding things, not taking your loot:** captives, territory, getting stronger in an area.
- **Persistent consequences while you're offline** *(idea)*: log in to find a companion kidnapped, or the fort smouldering after you raided the enemy's mines for ores one too many times. You respawn, resurrect townspeople, rebuild walls; the captive waits in the dungeon for you to retrieve.
- **A fort with defences you build up** *(idea)*.
- **Factions** *(idea)*: AI characters who can defect between factions; factions need upkeep; endgame about diplomacy and regional resources. Don't copy the Nemesis System — Warner Bros. holds a patent on it until 2036.
- **Captives and rescue:** a fallen companion left behind is taken, to be rescued later; the goblins took the smith *and* his hammer.
- **Set pieces for towns and the wilds** (Jeff, 2026-10-18: "start thinking about small and large town and wilds set pieces, well, wagon, cow, horse, trough, etc."): composed prop groups with a footprint and a parts list (a well yard, a wagon yard, a pasture with cow or horse and trough, a market corner, a smithy yard, a chapel corner, a wilds camp, a crossroads), in small-town and large-town sizes, placed as one piece by buildings' pass and by builder mode; new prop slots (wagon, trough, hitching post, hay, woodpile, coop, sty, shrine, milestone, cairn...) and farm animals as creature slots (cow, horse, chicken, pig, goat, dog) from the Quaternius pack where it has them. No gallows (Jeff, 2026-10-18). Art-kit leads the list with buildings, pc-character and economy (which animals work); a proposal to Jeff first, concepts from art-gen after he looks; infrastructure first, no real level changed until he asks.
- **Hauling:** physical loot you carry or drag home; carts on laid tracks for crates, Minecraft-style *(idea)*.

### Combat and bodies
- **Fighter controls** (from the combat discussion; layout B, Jeff 2026-10-15, decision 329): left click = quick swing; left hold-release = charged swing; right button = the off hand (a shield raises, a bow draws and looses on release; empty-handed it grapples, and a drag tosses); E tap opens what's beside you, E hold bandages.
- **Resources stay diegetic:** stamina (fighters), focus (archers/rogues), mind (casters); no bars. Mind is its own channel — body damage doesn't silence a caster; magic items and spells feed it.
- **Bandaging replaces passive regen** (done, decisions 272 and 320): E held on an adjacent ally (or yourself), out of combat, with a kit; the bandage sound, then mending starts and you can step away. A reason to break off from a fight. Companions bandage too.
- **Bodies come in a range:** heavy, lean, in between, for men and women alike, sharing one skeleton. Bigger = more mass (harder to shove, hits harder, slower) — Dire's point, and the physics already says so.
- **Body variation for monsters:** desynced animation phase and ±10% speed, ±12% scale with mass to match, colour variants, hood up/down, hand props (club, dagger, torch), temperament (bold, cautious, cowardly). Brutes visibly bigger than imps. Labels show just the kind ("Imp"), and only for monsters in sight.
- **Water physics:** crates float (walkable), boulders sink, creatures that fall in are slowed/stunned and can climb out; heavy ones drown.
- **Siege breathing room:** doors players can close and monsters can't open; monster respawns at the map edge/treeline, ≥12 cells from players, never inside walls; waves with lulls instead of constant respawns.

### Monster AI pass
- Now the `enemy-ai` worktree (see "Running in parallel").
- **Companion LOOK verb** (go check a dark or unseen spot and report back), built on the same sight machinery.

### Items
- **Item history:** now the `items` worktree (carriers with time held, up to five notable events).
- Later: items take a long-time holder's name ("Wren's dagger"); slowly absorb the holder's strength (a caster's staff gains mind) — capped, slow, drifts to a new owner; gifts between companions, townsfolk and players.
- *Ideas from Gísla saga:* cursed heirlooms (strong, but bring ill luck to whoever won't let go); reforging a broken heirloom into something new that keeps its name; a split token (two halves of a coin) carried by two friends.
- **Upkeep buffs:** food (mass), magic items (aura), social time like cards with a local (wits); found on the road too (larders, libraries, puzzles); companions mention when they fade, once.

### Companions
- **Card system:** random trait tables (birthplace, religion, parentage, profession, hobby, food preference, and so on) rolled at creation; one model call writes the card; a "how we met" field. *Idea from the saga:* good and bad omens/dreams.
- **World primer:** now the `lore` worktree (tone: *Gísla saga*).
- **Claude voice option** *(experiment)*: let a companion's voice run on the Claude API (Haiku/Sonnet) with fallback to the box's 14B; compare transcripts and cost. The `--voice-url` setting for a bigger local model already exists.
- **Promises match actions:** doors are real (in `companions`); "I'll check the chest" can stay roleplay.

### Maps, editor and outdoors
- **Editor:** F5 play from the editor; properties panel (chest contents, link destinations, start names) instead of hand-editing level.json; select/copy/paste; height painting; 3D preview.
- **Outdoors:** trees that block sight and slow you, tall grass that hides you, rocks and cliffs using height, streams with fords/bridges. Outdoor maps generated from a written composition (text → plan → height and terrain maps → rules pass → editor touch-up).. Done (505, 508, 513) except that the ground's heights are whole levels. Elevation (Jeff, 2026-10-19: "dirt having polys is not what i meant by elevation .. i meant hills and valleys and ridges"): real relief, rolling ground with hills, valleys and ridges, not stepped levels and not a faceted dirt patchwork; Design approved 2026-10-19 (maps and terrain, three landings): A, terrain's polys fix, the ground kinds read through a two-scale noise warp with a soft edge and grass wearing through, so patches stop following the cell grid; B, maps' Relief: an elevation per cell centre in centimetres (relief.png beside height.png), elevation derived from the whole levels where there is none so indoors and every existing level stay as they are, one rule (cliff_between) for the sim's steps and the drawn faces, walking where the rise is under 0.45 m with stairs and slopes to 0.5, uphill pace and route cost, falls by the drop, sight as a line in metres so a ridge hides the valley behind it, buildings on pads flat within 0.05 m, ponds level and streams stepping down, a generator (noise, ridge lines, flow-carved streams, pads) that is also the editor's fill, a height brush, protocol bump; levels/hills remade as a valley between rolling hills with a ridge, a gentle rise in the sample; C, terrain's drawing: the mesh from the corners with normals from the field, subdivided only where facets show, faces from the one rule, level water, things standing on the slope, the camera lifting over a ridge and following the ground, the castle drawn as today as the check. ### Engine, network and tooling
- **Keep the map bench** as a release check at 128² and 256², with budgets for tick time, load time and frame rate.
- **Staging server:** run a new build on a second port and check it before swapping it in.
- **Licensing records:** `THIRD_PARTY.md`, a `source` per asset with a test that fails without one, and dated license copies (e.g. DINOv3, 2025-08-19).
- **Goblin model touch-ups** when it matters in game: rest pose (stands upright like the mannequin), feet pointing forward.
- **The resource gate and mesh jobs** (Jeff, 2026-10-17, heavy jobs through the gate; the stalls of 2026-10-18): art-gen runs one TRELLIS mesh job at a time and queues none until free RAM covers its estimate plus the 8 GB margin, since a head out of reach held every tree's landing suite behind it (107 minutes on the day of v0.1.154, after 10 hours overnight from a nested take). Perf's rule: a job that fits passes an out-of-reach head (394 amended, 0b1575e4), and the head steps to the back of the queue itself (394 amended again, c052735d) so waiters started from an older heavy_job.py get past it too; art-gen's jobs run the main checkout's heavy_job.py, which release keeps at origin/main. A gated script never calls the gate itself (CLAUDE.md). Mesh jobs (perf, 2026-10-19): a TRELLIS run's 28.9 GB figure was commit, not RAM (Windows charges memory put on the card to system commit), so the gate now checks RAM and commit apart (394 amended a seventh time): TRELLIS wants about 9 GB of free RAM but 37 GB of free commit, and the commit limit (62 GB RAM plus a 38 GB pagefile on C:) leaves about 35 GB free while the sessions run, so mesh jobs fit only on a quiet machine; a 48–64 GB pagefile on D: is configured but not live until Windows restarts, Jeff's call, after which mesh jobs fit beside the usual load. Overruns (perf, approved 2026-10-19, after npc's soak with a parse error spun 7.6 hours under the gate): every gated run gets a limit (`--expect` minutes, else 3× the kind's recorded median with a 30-minute floor, else kinds.json max_minutes 90 for benches, suites, imports and exports); past it the job's own wrapper stops it and exits 5; a non-GPU job under 3% of a core for 10 minutes is stopped as hung; status shows "held N of M min"; long soaks pass `--expect`.

### Later
- **Music and ambience** (ambience now the `sound` worktree; music later): ambient beds first (wind, fire, birds); layered calm/tension/combat music per zone with stingers via Godot's interactive audio; ACE-Step (Apache 2.0, local) as the likely generator. Built 2026-10-22: maps' 585 with one tracer making every ledge from the relief's cliff edges (escarpments and plateaus drawn along smooth curves, hand-painted steps traced, the editor retracing on save; tops and feet per sample, taper points, a point at each fall, within 0.45 of the cliff edges, level_test checking both ways); terrain's side approved: each ledge's face a faceted strip along the curve from its top line to its foot line facing away from the up side, cell-edge faces a ledge covers no longer drawn, banks keeping theirs, the ground within reach drawn finer meeting the face's top on the up side and its foot below, the walk, the sight cut-out, the falls and the hillshade unchanged; view_test checks, before/after shots at the plateau and the fall's lip. Not needed yet.
- **Lighting pass:** dark ambient, light from torches and fires doing the work; nerve tied to light.
